<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove the four-dimensional disc embedding conjecture without a fundamental-group hypothesis, even when no homotopy classes or output framings are prescribed. We construct a compact oriented smooth four-manifold containing finitely many disc maps with framed algebraic dual spheres whose boundary circles bound no disjoint locally flat discs. Consequently, the free group on two generators is not good in the sense of Freedman–Quinn, and neither is any group containing it as a subgroup.

- **claim-boundary-filling-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold $M$ with nonempty boundary, an integer $k\geq1$, proper generically immersed discs $$f_i\colon(D^2,S^1)\longrightarrow(M,\partial M),\qquad 1\leq i\leq k,$$ whose boundary restrictions form an embedding of $\coprod_iS^1$, and framed sphere maps $g_i\colon S^2\to M$ such that $$\label{eq:input-invariants} \lambda(f_i,g_j)=\delta_{ij}\,1,\qquad \lambda(g_i,g_j)=0,\qquad \widetilde\mu(g_i)=0 \quad(1\leq i,j\leq k),$$ but the given boundary circles do not bound pairwise disjoint proper locally flat embedded discs in $M$. Each $g_i$ can be chosen to be a smooth embedded sphere; different $g_i$ are allowed to intersect.
  - Explanation: The constructed discs have framed algebraic dual spheres satisfying the displayed intersection conditions, yet their boundary circles admit no disjoint proper locally flat disc fillings. Each sphere is embedded individually; different spheres may intersect.
- **claim-free-group-nongoodness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-nongood)): The free group $F_2$ on two generators is not good in the sense of Freedman–Quinn. Every group containing a subgroup isomorphic to $F_2$ is likewise not good.
  - Explanation: Goodness of the nonabelian free group would pass through a finitely generated free subgroup and its quotient to the counterexample manifold's fundamental group. The forbidden filling contradicts that consequence.
- **claim-unrestricted-surgery-conjunction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-surgery-conjunction)): The unrestricted topological surgery and $s$-cobordism assertions cannot both hold.
  - Explanation: The unrestricted surgery and cobordism assertions jointly imply the excluded disc filling. The corollary rejects their conjunction without identifying which assertion fails.
- **claim-graph-and-peripheral-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-graph)): The manifold $Q$ is connected and $$\pi_1(Q)=F(h_0,h_+,h_-,z_a\ (a\in\mathcal E)), \qquad [\ell_a]=z_a,\quad [m_a]=1.$$ The manifold $G$ has the homotopy type of the rose $\Gamma$ on $h_0,h_+,h_-,q_1,\ldots,q_d$. There is a homotopy equivalence $p_0:G\longrightarrow\Gamma$ that sends $V$ into the $h$-subrose $\Gamma_h$, induces the named generators on $h$, kills the end generators, and on each $C_e$ is its interval coordinate traversing $q_e$ once.
  - Explanation: The exterior has the stated free peripheral group. The ball attachments kill the end generators while introducing graph loops, and the specified boundary coordinate map extends to a homotopy equivalence.
- **claim-framed-ribbon-operation** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-framing)): Given two disjoint oriented framed knots in the interior of $Q$ and a relative path class joining them, one can construct a punctured torus in a level of a collar $Q\times[0,\epsilon)$ whose two basis curves resolve normally to these knots. Its oriented boundary represents their commutator, with the change of basing given by the chosen path. The two band framings can be varied by arbitrary integers independently. The resolved knots admit disjoint rising annuli to the given framed knots, and a unit band twist changes the relative oriented normal framing of its annulus by a unit integer.
  - Explanation: A ribbon realizes the prescribed commutator and basing path. Independent band twists adjust the ordered normal framings transported by the rising annuli.
- **claim-simultaneous-capped-stages** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-stages)): For every $r\in\mathcal R$ there is a punctured torus $B_r$ properly embedded in $V$, with boundary a knot in $\operatorname{int}Q$. The $B_r$ are mutually disjoint. They have intersecting basis curves $\alpha_r,\beta_r$ and the following attachments in $G$:* 1. *A shallow cap at $\alpha_r$ ends in a separate parallel copy of the core disc of end $a$.* 2. *A further punctured torus $S_r$ is attached at $\beta_r$. Its basis curves $x_r,y_r$ have caps of end types $b,c$, respectively. Its boundary, with the prescribed basing, represents $[z_b^{c_2},z_c^{c_3}]$, and $\partial B_r$ represents $R_r$.* 3. *Compressing $S_r$ along $x_r$ with two framed parallels of its cap produces an embedded disc $D_r$. Intersections of $D_r$ with other objects are allowed. The extension of $D_r$ towards $\beta_r$ has a framing that extends the angular normal-circle vector required to compress $\beta_r\times S^1$.* *Except at the intended attachments, every body and attaching collar is disjoint from every other such piece. All other intersections are transverse crossings of opposite end cap sheets in the product charts <a href="#geo:product-sheets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:product-sheets">[geo:product-sheets]</a>.
  - Explanation: Separated collar levels realize the entire finite relation family with disjoint bodies and attaching collars. Upper-stage compression produces embedded contracting discs with the framing needed for the later torus compression; crossings with other objects remain permitted.
- **claim-embedded-framed-dual-candidates** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-dual-candidates)): Each $g_r$ is individually embedded and has trivial normal bundle. It follows that $$\lambda(g_r,g_r)=0,\qquad \widetilde\mu(g_r)=0.$$ The compression leaves the single product-torus intersection with $f_r$ unchanged. Every other intersection involving an $f_r$ and a $g_s$, or two distinct spheres, occurs on the cap sheets in <a href="#geo:product-sheets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:product-sheets">[geo:product-sheets]</a>.
  - Explanation: Each compressed sphere is embedded and has trivial normal bundle. Its diagonal equivariant pairing and reduced self-intersection invariant vanish, and compression preserves the intended disc intersection.
- **claim-exterior-wall-label-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-labels)): Within one contracting-disc copy on a sphere, pair the two oppositely oriented $x$-cap sheets at their crossings with any fixed opposite-type sheet. The paired intersections have equal Wall labels in $\pi_1(M)$ and opposite signs. All cap-sheet contributions to $\lambda(f_r,g_s)$ and to $\lambda(g_r,g_s)$ for $r\ne s$ therefore cancel.
  - Explanation: The unused upper cap kills the comparison loop in the exterior itself. Paired crossings therefore have equal group labels and opposite signs, so cancellation holds in the full equivariant pairings.
- **claim-exact-disc-embedding-input** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-input-invariants)): There are orientations and whiskers for the discs and spheres such that, for all $r,s\in\mathcal R$ and $R=\mathbb Z[\pi_1(M)]$, $$\label{geo:invariants} \lambda(f_r,g_s)=\delta_{rs}\,1\in R, \qquad \lambda(g_r,g_s)=0\in R, \qquad \widetilde\mu(g_r)=0.$$ In particular the normal second Stiefel–Whitney number of every $g_r$ is zero. These data satisfy all algebraic and boundary hypotheses of the unrestricted disc-embedding assertion.
  - Explanation: Orientations and whiskers normalize the surviving diagonal coefficient and preserve all zero entries. The resulting discs and spheres satisfy the unrestricted assertion's algebraic and boundary requirements.
- **claim-protected-disc-smooth-cuts** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-topological-cuts)): Suppose there are pairwise disjoint proper locally flat embeddings $\overline f_r:D^2\longrightarrow M$ with $\overline f_r\|_{S^1}=f_r\|_{S^1}$. Then $p_0$ is homotopic, relative to $\partial G$, to a map $p:G\longrightarrow\Gamma$ with the following properties. The bases, the short return annuli in their deleted tubes, and the disc images lie in $p^{-1}(\Gamma_h)$. For one interior point of each $q_e$ edge, the inverse image $J_e$ is a compact smooth oriented three-manifold with exactly one boundary torus. Additional closed components of $J_e$ are allowed.
  - Explanation: Actual disjoint locally flat disc images permit a boundary-fixed homotopy of the graph map. Regular levels away from those protected images produce smooth walls with the stated torus boundaries; the discs themselves are not smoothed.
- **claim-geometric-input-forced-cut-data** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-geo-construction)): For every $d\geq5$, with the finite word set $\mathcal H$ defined above, the preceding construction produces a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold $M$ with nonempty boundary, a finite family $F=(f_r)_{r\in\mathcal R}$ of smooth proper disc maps with disjoint embedded boundary, and individually embedded framed sphere maps $(g_r)_{r\in\mathcal R}$ satisfying <a href="#geo:invariants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:invariants">[geo:invariants]</a>.* *If the circles $\partial F$ bound pairwise disjoint proper locally flat embedded discs in $M$, the following data exist:* 1. *The compact oriented smooth four-manifold $G$ has a homotopy equivalence $p:G\longrightarrow\Gamma$, where $\Gamma$ is the rose on $h_0,h_+,h_-,q_1,\ldots,q_d$. The boundary is $Q\cup\bigcup_e C_e$, with the free group and peripheral coordinates of Lemma <a href="#geo:graph" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:graph">4</a> and the longitude–meridian interchange <a href="#geo:hopf-collar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:hopf-collar">[geo:hopf-collar]</a>.* 2. *There are disjoint cooriented smooth cuts $J_1,\ldots,J_d$, each a compact oriented three-manifold with one boundary torus and possibly additional closed components. Their boundary tori are the fixed product cuts in the $C_e$.* 3. *Cutting $G$ along all $J_e$ gives a compact oriented smooth four-manifold $X$, possibly disconnected. With half collars absorbed into $Q$, its boundary is obtained by gluing $Q$ to the two oppositely oriented copies of each $J_e$ along their boundary tori. Every torus-bearing wall component has just that one torus; closed wall components remain separate. Regluing the paired cuts recovers $G$.* 4. *The map on $X$ lands in the graph cut open at the chosen $q_e$ points, namely $\Gamma_h$ with the resulting half-edges; it is constant on every copy of a cut at its endpoint value. All words <a href="#geo:relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:relations">[geo:relations]</a> are trivial in the fundamental group of the component $X_Q$ containing $Q$, with the original basings in $Q$.* 5. *Extend <a href="#geo:matrices" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:matrices">[geo:matrices]</a> to $\pi_1(\Gamma)$ by $\rho(q_e)=1$. The associated adjoint local system on $G$ restricts to $\rho$ on the $h$ generators, to the identity on all $z_a$, and to a trivial system on each $J_e$, with matching reference frames on its two copies induced by the graph point.* *The implication uses only the asserted embedded discs. It imposes neither output-dual conditions nor an additional framing or relative homotopy condition on those discs.
  - Explanation: The finite construction supplies the required algebraic duals. A hypothetical boundary filling additionally forces smooth cuts, based triple relations in the component containing the exterior, and compatible reference local systems, without prescribing output framings or relative homotopy classes.
- **claim-paired-mayer-vietoris** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-paired-mv)): Let $$W_h=\operatorname{im}\bigl(H^1(\Gamma_h;\mathfrak l_\rho) \longrightarrow H^1(X;\mathfrak l_\rho)\bigr).$$ Here the map uses the retraction of the cut-open graph onto its $h$-rose. For either degree, write $r_i^\pm$ for restriction to the two copies of $J_i$. The map $$\Delta(u,v)= \bigl(r_i^+u-r_i^-v,\ r_i^-u-r_i^+v\bigr)_{i=1}^d \label{def:delta}$$ is onto in degree one, with kernel $W_h\oplus W_h$, and is an isomorphism in degree two. Consequently, if $H^1(X;\mathfrak l_\rho)=W_h\oplus U$, then $$\Delta:U\oplus U\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ } \bigoplus_i\bigl(H^1(J_i;\mathfrak l) \oplus H^1(J_i;\mathfrak l)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The graph cover makes the paired restrictions surjective in degree one with the stated graph-derived kernel and invertible in degree two. Removing the kernel gives the complementary restriction isomorphism.
- **claim-slopes-complementary-internal-directions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-slopes)): The ends of each cut can be designated $+$ and $-$, and torus coordinates $(\theta_i,\eta_i)$ of period one can be chosen, so that the image of $$H^1(J_i;\mathbb C)\longrightarrow H^1(\partial J_i;\mathbb C)$$ is the $\theta_i$-axis. In $Q$, the $\eta_i$-circle bounds on the positive side and the $\theta_i$-circle bounds on the negative side. Put $$n_i=\dim_{\mathbb C}H^1(J_i,\partial J_i;\mathfrak l), \qquad D=\sum_i n_i, \qquad \mathcal Y=\bigoplus_i(\mathbb C^{n_i}\oplus\mathbb C^{n_i}).$$ Then $\dim U=3d+D$. The joint first-slope restriction $U\to\mathfrak l^d$ is onto. Its kernel $U_0$ has dimension $D$. The two maps from $U_0$ to $\mathcal Y$ given by the paired restrictions and by their interchange are injective and have complementary images.
  - Explanation: Boundary duality identifies the restriction line with a peripheral axis. The paired restriction isomorphism then yields independent first-slope coordinates and complementary internal tangent images, including the contributions of closed wall components.
- **claim-minimal-defect-equation-charts** (construction, [source](#stmt-def-equation-chart)): Let $Z$ be a compact smooth manifold, possibly disconnected and with boundary, and let $\rho_Z$ be a fixed $\operatorname{SL}_2(\mathbb C)$-local system. Choose a subspace $T\subset H^1(Z;\mathfrak l_{\rho_Z})$. There is an algebraic family of edge matrices on a finite good cover, with parameters $T$, having these properties:* 1. *Its reference value is the chosen local system, its tangent is the specified inclusion of $T$, and its triangle-defect ideal $\mathfrak r_Z$ is contained in $\mathfrak m^2$.* 2. *Modulo $\mathfrak r_Z$, its matrices are the transitions of an exact flat family.* 3. *For a nonzero functional $\lambda:\mathfrak r_Z/\mathfrak m\mathfrak r_Z\to\mathbb C$, let $\mathfrak s_\lambda\subset\mathfrak r_Z$ be the inverse image of $\ker\lambda$. The specified flat family over $B/\mathfrak r_Z$, where $B=\mathbb C[[T]]$, has no flat lift over $B/\mathfrak s_\lambda$. This excludes compatible general-linear lifts as well, and does not require a putative lift to remain in the parameter slice $T$.
  - Explanation: Solving the independent linear defect equations leaves an algebraic chart with the chosen tangent slice and retains every higher defect. Each nonzero functional on the minimal defect layer obstructs arbitrary compatible flat lifts, including general-linear lifts outside the slice.
- **claim-algebraic-wall-vertex-restrictions** (construction, [source](#stmt-def-restriction-charts)): There are wall charts with parameters $$(a_i,y_i)\in\mathbb C^3\oplus\mathbb C^{n_i},$$ vertex parameters $(a,t)\in\mathbb C^{3d}\oplus\mathbb C^D$, a vertex defect ideal $\mathfrak r\subset\mathbb C[[a,t]]$, and algebraic maps $$\iota_i^+(a,t)=(a_i,Y_i^+(a,t)),\qquad \iota_i^-(a,t)=(0,Y_i^-(a,t)), \label{def:restriction-maps}$$ with the following properties. The maps give the actual based restrictions of the flat vertex family modulo $\mathfrak r$. The $a_i$ are algebraic group coordinates for the first-slope holonomy, centered at the identity. The two maps $$t\longmapsto Y(0,t),\qquad t\longmapsto \sigma Y(0,t), \quad Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d,$$ have injective and complementary tangent images in $\mathcal Y$.
  - Explanation: Based restriction words give algebraic maps between the charts. Actual first-slope coordinates separate the positive and negative restrictions, and the internal restriction graphs have injective complementary tangents.
- **claim-finite-adjoint-spanning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-spanning)): For the word set $\mathcal H$ fixed in Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">2</a> and the representation in <a href="#geo:matrices" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:matrices">[geo:matrices]</a>, one has $$\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb C} \{\operatorname{Ad}\rho(c):c\in\mathcal H\} =\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb C}(\mathfrak l).$$
  - Explanation: Spectral projections of the diagonal adjoint action and the unipotent adjoint actions generate all matrix units. The prescribed finite word set therefore spans the entire endomorphism algebra.
- **claim-end-holonomy-cubic-nilpotence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-cubic-nilpotence)): In $B/\mathfrak r$, where $B=\mathbb C[[a,t]]$, let $\mathfrak n$ be generated by all matrix entries of the end holonomies minus $1$. Then $$\mathfrak n^3=0. \label{def:n-cube}$$ In particular, for either restriction map in <a href="#def:restriction-maps" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="def:restriction-maps">[def:restriction-maps]</a>, $$(\iota_i^\pm)^*(\mathfrak k_i)^3\subset\mathfrak r . \label{def:k-cube}$$ The same conclusion holds for the cube of the sum of all these pulled-back ideals.
  - Explanation: Independent conjugators make the leading triple commutators detect every scalar cubic product of end deviations. Nakayama's lemma makes their ideal cube vanish, and all pulled-back wall ideals lie in that same deviation ideal.
- **claim-formal-image-bundle** (construction, [source](#stmt-conn-image-bundle)): For a finite algebraic transition-cochain chart on a compact smooth manifold $Z$, let $\mathfrak d$ be its triangle-defect ideal. There is a smooth formal rank-two bundle and connection whose reduction modulo $\mathfrak d$ is the specified flat family. Its fiber curvature lies in the closed extension of $\mathfrak d$. The construction permits a total connection including parameter directions. If the reference bundle is trivial, it has a global formal frame extending any chosen reference frame.
  - Explanation: A corrected formal idempotent turns the transition cochain into a genuine smooth formal bundle. Blended local connections recover the specified flat reduction and place the fiber curvature in the defect ideal.
- **claim-prescribed-wall-collar** (construction, [source](#stmt-conn-wall-collar)): For a wall chart $(a_i,y_i)$, the connection in Lemma <a href="#conn:image-bundle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conn:image-bundle">20</a> can be chosen, in a formal frame on a torus collar, to have total connection form $$U_i(a_i)\,d\theta_i+V_i(a_i,y_i)\,d\eta_i , \label{conn:collar}$$ with zero parameter components. It still reduces to the given flat family modulo $\mathfrak d_i$. The collar frame extends to a global formal frame on $J_i$ with $A_i(0)=0$.
  - Explanation: Formal logarithms prescribe the fiber connection on each torus collar while parameter components vanish there. The frame extends globally and preserves the flat reduction. The off-equation logarithms need not commute.
- **claim-polarized-variation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-variation)): Writing $F_A=dA+A\wedge A$, every parameter variation satisfies $$\delta P_i =2\int_{J_i}\operatorname{tr}(\delta A_i\wedge F_{A_i}) +2\epsilon_i\operatorname{tr}(V_i\,\delta U_i). \label{conn:variation-formula}$$ Consequently $P_i\in\mathfrak m_i^3$ and every coefficient of its parameter differential lies in $\mathfrak k_i\mathbb C[[a_i,y_i]]$.
  - Explanation: The polarization cancels the unwanted boundary variation. Curvature and the second-slope logarithm control every parameter derivative, giving cubic order of the potential and gradient membership in the wall ideal.
- **claim-traceless-vertex-curvature** (construction, [source](#stmt-conn-vertex-connection)): The vertex chart admits a global formal connection $A_X$ whose curvature is traceless and belongs to $\mathfrak r$. It reduces to the exact transition family modulo $\mathfrak r$. Its reference bundle is trivial.
  - Explanation: The graph-derived reference bundle is trivial. Correcting the determinant connection gives a global formal connection with traceless curvature in the vertex defect ideal and the prescribed flat reduction.
- **claim-gradient-generates-full-defect-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-derivative-ideal)): In $B=\mathbb C[[a,t]]$, $$\bigl(\partial_C\Psi(a,Y(a,t))\bigr)=\mathfrak r. \label{conn:derivative-identity}$$
  - Explanation: Internal wall derivatives detect each restricted curvature obstruction by relative duality. Paired degree-two restrictions then detect the vertex obstruction, and the minimal defect-layer property excludes an annihilating functional. Nakayama's lemma yields equality of the whole ideals.
- **claim-closed-chern-simons-comparisons** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-closed-comparisons)): Let $N$ be a closed oriented three-manifold, and let $A$ be a global formal connection with $F_A\in I$, for a scalar ideal $I$.* 1. *If $\eta\in I$ is a one-form, then $$\operatorname{CS}_N(A+\eta)-\operatorname{CS}_N(A)\in I^2.$$* 2. *For an arbitrary smooth formal gauge $g$, $$\operatorname{CS}_N(A^g)-\operatorname{CS}_N(A)$$ is independent of the parameters. The reference gauge need not be nullhomotopic.
  - Explanation: When both curvature and the connection perturbation lie in the scalar ideal, their Chern–Simons difference lies in its square. A separate gauge change contributes only a parameter constant, even for a nontrivial reference gauge homotopy class.
- **claim-square-ideal-potential-value** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-value-ideal)): For the same maps and potential, $$\Psi(a,Y(a,t))\in\mathfrak r^2 . \label{conn:value-identity}$$
  - Explanation: Pure collar connections glue with an exactly flat exterior connection. Connection perturbations contribute only the square ideal, gauge terms disappear after reference subtraction, and Stokes identifies the remaining boundary value with curvature squared.
- **claim-fixed-ring-gradient-jets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-all-jets)): For each wall there is one fixed algebraic chart ring $A_i^*$, with completion $\mathbb C[[a_i,y_i]]$, containing the original transition germs and $\mathfrak k_i$, such that for every integer $b\geq1$, every coefficient of $d_{\mathrm{param}}P_i$ modulo $\mathfrak k_i^b$ is the image of an element of $A_i^*$. The ring is independent of $b$.
  - Explanation: Controlled local frames, truncated logarithms and explicit inverse expansions represent every finite gradient jet in a single complex algebraic chart ring. This does not assert algebraicity of the full potential or a common integer coefficient ring for its original series.
- **claim-deformation-output-cubic-laws** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conn-output)): The algebraic maps in Proposition <a href="#def:restriction-charts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:restriction-charts">17</a> and the formal functions $P_i\in\mathbb C[[a_i,y_i]]$ have the following properties:* 1. *$P_i\in\mathfrak m_i^3$, its gradient is zero modulo $\mathfrak k_i$, and all its gradient jets have representatives in one fixed complex algebraic chart ring as in Proposition <a href="#conn:all-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conn:all-jets">28</a>.* 2. *The two tangent images of $Y(0,t)$ and $\sigma Y(0,t)$ are complementary $D$-planes in the $2D$-dimensional $C$-space.* 3. *For $\Psi$ in <a href="#conn:psi" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="conn:psi">[conn:psi]</a>, $$\bigl(\partial_C\Psi(a,Y)\bigr)=\mathfrak r,\qquad \Psi(a,Y)\in\mathfrak r^2 .$$* 4. *Choose one scalar first-slope coordinate $a_{i,1}$ for each $i$, and define $$p_i(y_i)=\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i(0,y_i). \label{conn:p-functions}$$ For every triple of labels, including repetitions, $$a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1},\qquad p_i(Y_i^-)\,p_j(Y_j^-)\,p_k(Y_k^-) \ \in\ \bigl(\partial_C\Psi(a,Y)\bigr). \label{conn:cubic-laws}$$* *Moreover the cube of the sum of the pulled-back wall ideals is contained in $\mathfrak r$, as in Proposition <a href="#def:cubic-nilpotence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:cubic-nilpotence">19</a>.
  - Explanation: The deformation output combines complementary tangents, fixed-ring gradient jets, the full derivative ideal and square-value identity. Both first-slope coordinates and negative-wall first-slope derivatives inherit the end-deviation cubic law.
- **claim-formal-arc-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-arc)): Let $S$ be a reduced complete Noetherian local $\mathbb C$-algebra with residue field $\mathbb C$. Every nonzero element $b$ of its maximal ideal has nonzero image under some continuous local $\mathbb C$-algebra homomorphism $S\longrightarrow\mathbb C[[u]]$. Consequently, if $I\subset B=\mathbb C[[v_1,\ldots,v_n]]$ is radical and $P\in B$ satisfies $\partial_{v_j}P\in I$ for every $j$, then $P-P(0)\in I$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero maximal-ideal element survives on a formal arc through a reduced complete local complex algebra. The chain rule then forces a primitive with gradient in a radical ideal to be constant modulo that ideal.
- **claim-algebraic-primitive-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-replacement)): Let $A$ be the local ring at a complex point of an étale neighborhood of affine $n$-space, with coordinates $v$ centered at that point, and let $B=\widehat A=\mathbb C[[v]]$. Let $K$ be any ideal of $A$. Suppose $P\in B$ satisfies $$\partial_{v_j}P\in KB,\qquad \partial_{v_j}P\in A+K^bB \quad\text{for every $j$ and every $b\geq1$}. \label{alg:replacement-hyp}$$ For every $m\geq1$ there is $P_m\in A$ such that $P-P_m\in K^mB$. No reducedness, primaryness, or equidimensionality condition is imposed on $K$.
  - Explanation: All-order algebraic representatives of the gradient descend a whole finite Taylor tuple through faithful flatness. Differential tests at every primary component make a sufficiently long tuple control the desired ideal power, including embedded components.
- **claim-replacement-preserves-membership-witnesses** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-replacement-preserves)): For the data of Definition <a href="#alg:input-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:input-data">31</a>, one can replace each $P_i^{\mathrm{o}}$ by an algebraic germ $P_i$ with $P_i-P_i^{\mathrm{o}}\in\mathfrak k_i^9B_i$. The $P_i$ still have order at least three. Define $$\Psi(a,C)=\sum_i\bigl(P_i(a_i,C_i^+)-P_i(0,C_i^-)\bigr), \qquad p_i(y_i)=\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i(0,y_i),$$ and, as columns and scalars in $B_X$, set $$v=(\partial_C\Psi)(a,Y),\qquad g=\Psi(a,Y), \qquad \nu_i=p_i(Y_i^-).$$ Then $(v)=\mathfrak r$, $g\in\mathfrak r^2$, and both types of triple products in <a href="#alg:triple-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:triple-input">[alg:triple-input]</a> belong to $(v)$ with the new $p_i$. Moreover, there are algebraic germs $b_{jl}$ and row vectors $h_+^{ijk},h_-^{ijk}$, in one common local étale chart in $z$, such that $$g=v^{\mathsf T}bv,\qquad a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}=h_+^{ijk}v,\qquad \nu_i\nu_j\nu_k=h_-^{ijk}v. \label{alg:algebraic-witnesses}$$ Here $b$ is a $2D$-by-$2D$ matrix, with no symmetry requirement.
  - Explanation: High wall-ideal order preserves cubic order, the square-value containment and full gradient generation. Faithful flatness then supplies algebraic coefficients witnessing the value identity and both families of cubic memberships.
- **claim-finite-coefficient-ring** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-finite-ring)): Given finitely many algebraic germs in finitely many coordinate spaces over $\mathbb C$, and finitely many specified nonzero complex constants, there is a finitely generated $\mathbb Z$-subalgebra $R\subset\mathbb C$ containing every coefficient of those germs and the inverses of the specified constants.
  - Explanation: Finite étale presentations and inverse initial Jacobians place all coefficients of the selected algebraic germs in a finitely generated integer subalgebra. Degree recursion introduces no growing family of numerical denominators.
- **claim-denominator-free-remainders** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-remainder)): Let $R$ be a commutative ring and $\Phi(a,C)\in(a,C)^3R[[a,C]]$, where $C$ has $N$ entries. There is an $N$-by-$N$ matrix $H(a,C,\delta)$ over $R[[a,C,\delta]]$, with every entry in $(a,C,\delta)$, such that $$\Phi(a,C+\delta) =\Phi(a,C)+(\partial_C\Phi)(a,C)^{\mathsf T}\delta +\delta^{\mathsf T}H(a,C,\delta)\delta. \label{alg:remainder-formula}$$ There is also a matrix $G(a,C,\delta)$ with positive-order entries satisfying $$(\partial_C\Phi)(a,C+\delta)-(\partial_C\Phi)(a,C) =G(a,C,\delta)\delta. \label{alg:gradient-difference}$$ Both constructions use only addition, multiplication, and integer binomial coefficients. The difference of any formal scalar function at $C+\delta$ and $C$ similarly factors as a row times $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Monomial binomial expansion gives a quadratic remainder and a positive-order gradient-difference factorization over any commutative coefficient ring. Neither construction divides by numerical factorials.
- **claim-corrected-formal-data** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-formal-data)): The input in Definition <a href="#alg:input-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:input-data">31</a> yields a finitely generated $\mathbb Z$-subalgebra $R\subset\mathbb C$, separated potentials $$\begin{gathered} P_i\in(a_i,y_i)^3R[[a_i,y_i]],\qquad p_i(y_i)=\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i(0,y_i),\\ \Psi(a,C)=\sum_i\bigl(P_i(a_i,C_i^+)-P_i(0,C_i^-)\bigr), \end{gathered}$$ and a centered formal map $\widetilde Y(a,t)\in(z)R[[z]]^{2D}$ with the same first-order part as the original $Y$. With $\widetilde v=(\partial_C\Psi)(a,\widetilde Y)$, there are row vectors $\widetilde h_+^{ijk},\widetilde h_-^{ijk}$ over $R[[z]]$ such that, for every ordered triple of labels, $$\begin{split} \Psi(a,\widetilde Y)&=0,\\ a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}&=\widetilde h_+^{ijk}\widetilde v,\\ p_i(\widetilde Y_i^-) p_j(\widetilde Y_j^-) p_k(\widetilde Y_k^-)&=\widetilde h_-^{ijk}\widetilde v. \end{split} \label{alg:seven}$$ The determinant $\det[\partial_t\widetilde Y(0,0)\ \sigma\partial_t\widetilde Y(0,0)]$ is a unit of $R$. In particular $\widetilde Y(0,t)$ and $\sigma\widetilde Y(0,t)$ parametrize smooth formal submanifolds of the $C$-space with complementary tangent spaces. On both submanifolds $F(C)=\Psi(0,C)$ vanishes identically. The same ring $R$ contains the coefficients of the potentials, the corrected map, and all the displayed membership witnesses.
  - Explanation: A contracting matrix recursion corrects the evaluation graph until the separated potential vanishes exactly. An invertible change of gradient generators transports both cubic laws, preserving complementary tangents and a common coefficient ring for the graph and all witnesses.
- **claim-large-characteristic-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-specialization)): For every sufficiently large rational prime $\ell$, the ring $R$ in Theorem <a href="#alg:formal-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:formal-data">38</a> has a homomorphism to a finite field $\Bbbk$ of characteristic $\ell$. Reducing every coefficient by such a homomorphism gives separated potentials of order at least three, the identities <a href="#alg:seven" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:seven">[alg:seven]</a> with their witnesses, and the two complementary zero graphs over $\Bbbk$. In particular $\ell$ can be chosen larger than any prescribed finite bound.
  - Explanation: A finite arithmetic model specializes the new potentials, corrected graph and membership witnesses to finite fields of sufficiently large characteristic. The inverse tangent determinant survives; the original integration-defined series are not specialized.
- **claim-frobenius-formal-data-nonexistence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frob-no-data)): Let $\Bbbk$ be a field of prime characteristic $\ell>2$, and let $d\geq5$. For $1\leq i\leq d$, choose an integer $n_i\geq0$, a tuple $a_i=(a_{i,1},a_{i,2},a_{i,3})$ of variables, and a tuple $y_i$ of $n_i$ variables. Put $D=\sum_i n_i$, write $a=(a_1,\ldots,a_d)$, and let $C=(C_i^+,C_i^-)_{i=1}^d$ be $2D$ variables, with $n_i$ variables in each indicated block. There do not exist series $$P_i\in(a_i,y_i)^3\Bbbk[[a_i,y_i]],\qquad Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d\in (a,t)\Bbbk[[a,t]]^{2D},\qquad t=(t_1,\ldots,t_D),$$ having the following properties. Define $$\Psi(a,C)=\sum_{i=1}^d \bigl(P_i(a_i,C_i^+)-P_i(0,C_i^-)\bigr),\qquad p_i(y_i)=(\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i)(0,y_i), \label{frob:separated}$$ and let $\sigma$ interchange the $+$ and $-$ blocks for each $i$. All indicated derivatives are taken before substitution. First, the two linear maps $$A=\partial_tY(0,0),\qquad \sigma A: \Bbbk^D\longrightarrow\Bbbk^{2D}$$ have complementary images and are injective, or equivalently the square matrix $[A\ \sigma A]$ is invertible. Second, $$\Psi(a,Y(a,t))=0. \label{frob:graph-zero}$$ Finally, for every $i,j,k$ (including repetitions), both memberships $$\begin{split} a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}&\in J_Y,\\ p_i(Y_i^-(a,t))p_j(Y_j^-(a,t))p_k(Y_k^-(a,t))&\in J_Y, \qquad J_Y=\bigl((\partial_{C_\nu}\Psi)(a,Y(a,t))\bigr)_{\nu=1}^{2D} \end{split} \label{frob:memberships}$$ hold in $\Bbbk[[a,t]]$.
  - Explanation: Separated potentials cannot simultaneously possess the specified complementary zero graph and both cubic gradient-ideal laws in the stated prime-characteristic regime. The proof pairs an explicitly lifted tensor cycle with a moving normal form to contradict a nonzero parameter product.
- **claim-normal-cycles-restricted-memberships** (construction, [source](#stmt-frob-normal-cycle)): Suppose that $\Gamma$ is a formal smooth $D$-dimensional graph in the $C$ variables, centered at the origin, with $F\|_\Gamma=0$. Complete a parametrization to formal coordinates $(t,u)$, with $\Gamma$ given by $u_1=\cdots=u_D=0$. In $\Omega$ its normal form $$\gamma=\left(\prod_{b=1}^D u_b^{\ell-1}\right) \mathrm du_1\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm du_D \label{frob:normal-form}$$ is annihilated by the normal ideal $(u)$ and satisfies $\mathrm d\gamma=\delta\gamma=0$. If an ambient function $g$ has a restricted expression $$g\|_\Gamma=\sum_{\nu=1}^{2D}h_\nu(t) (\partial_{C_\nu}F)\|_\Gamma, \label{frob:restricted-membership}$$ then $g\gamma$ is a Koszul boundary. More precisely, lift $h_\nu$ to $\widetilde h_\nu(C)$ by the inverse tangential coordinates and let $\iota_\nu$ be contraction with $\partial/\partial C_\nu$. Then $$g\gamma=\delta\left(\sum_\nu \widetilde h_\nu\iota_\nu\gamma\right). \label{frob:primitive-general}$$ If two such graphs have complementary tangent spaces, their normal forms have nonzero residue pairing.
  - Explanation: In the coordinate Frobenius quotient, the graph normal form is closed for both differentials and killed by its normal ideal. Restricted gradient memberships give explicit ambient Koszul primitives, and complementary graphs have nonzero residue pairing.
- **claim-chain-retraction-with-homotopy** (construction, [source](#stmt-frob-retraction)): Let $(V,\mathcal D)$ be a finite-dimensional graded complex over a field, and let $S:V\to V$ have degree zero. No relation between $S$ and $\mathcal D$ is assumed. Set $$N=\ker S,\qquad K=N\cap\mathcal D^{-1}N.$$ There are a degree-zero chain idempotent $r$ and a degree-minus-one map $h$ such that $$r(N)=K,\qquad r\|_K=\operatorname{id},\qquad 1-r=\mathcal Dh+h\mathcal D. \label{frob:retraction-identity}$$ The image of $r$ is not asserted to equal $K$.
  - Explanation: A complement of the differential-stable part of the endomorphism kernel determines a contractible pair and an explicit homotopy. The resulting chain idempotent sends that kernel onto its differential-stable part, but its entire image need not equal that part.
- **claim-joint-tensor-kernels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-frob-tensor-kernel)): For finite-dimensional vector spaces $V_i$ and endomorphisms $S_i$, put $N_i=\ker S_i$, and let $W$ be an additional tensor factor on which all $S_i$ act as the identity. On $V_1\otimes\cdots\otimes V_d\otimes W$ one has $$\bigcap_{\|I\|=3}\ker\left(\prod_{i\in I}S_i\right) =\sum_{\|J\|\geq d-2} \left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^d V_i(J)\right)\otimes W, \qquad V_i(J)=\begin{cases}N_i&i\in J,\\ V_i&i\notin J.\end{cases} \label{frob:tensor-kernel-identity}$$ This also holds with additional passive factors interspersed in any fixed tensor order.
  - Explanation: Splitting each factor into its kernel and a complementary domain reduces the joint kernel to the stated sum of tensor subspaces. Injectivity on the complementary domain suffices even when its image intersects the kernel.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-topological-cuts",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose there are pairwise disjoint proper locally flat embeddings $\\overline f_r:D^2\\longrightarrow M$ with $\\overline f_r|_{S^1}=f_r|_{S^1}$. Then $p_0$ is homotopic, relative to $\\partial G$, to a map $p:G\\longrightarrow\\Gamma$ with the following properties. The bases, the short return annuli in their deleted tubes, and the disc images lie in $p^{-1}(\\Gamma_h)$. For one interior point of each $q_e$ edge, the inverse image $J_e$ is a compact smooth oriented three-manifold with exactly one boundary torus. Additional closed components of $J_e$ are allowed.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-construction",
        "exact_quote": "For every $d\\geq5$, with the finite word set $\\mathcal H$ defined above, the preceding construction produces a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold $M$ with nonempty boundary, a finite family $F=(f_r)_{r\\in\\mathcal R}$ of smooth proper disc maps with disjoint embedded boundary, and individually embedded framed sphere maps $(g_r)_{r\\in\\mathcal R}$ satisfying <a href=\"#geo:invariants\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:invariants\">[geo:invariants]</a>.*\n\n*If the circles $\\partial F$ bound pairwise disjoint proper locally flat embedded discs in $M$, the following data exist:*\n\n1.  *The compact oriented smooth four-manifold $G$ has a homotopy equivalence $p:G\\longrightarrow\\Gamma$, where $\\Gamma$ is the rose on $h_0,h_+,h_-,q_1,\\ldots,q_d$. The boundary is $Q\\cup\\bigcup_e C_e$, with the free group and peripheral coordinates of Lemma <a href=\"#geo:graph\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:graph\">4</a> and the longitude–meridian interchange <a href=\"#geo:hopf-collar\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:hopf-collar\">[geo:hopf-collar]</a>.*\n\n2.  *There are disjoint cooriented smooth cuts $J_1,\\ldots,J_d$, each a compact oriented three-manifold with one boundary torus and possibly additional closed components. Their boundary tori are the fixed product cuts in the $C_e$.*\n\n3.  *Cutting $G$ along all $J_e$ gives a compact oriented smooth four-manifold $X$, possibly disconnected. With half collars absorbed into $Q$, its boundary is obtained by gluing $Q$ to the two oppositely oriented copies of each $J_e$ along their boundary tori. Every torus-bearing wall component has just that one torus; closed wall components remain separate. Regluing the paired cuts recovers $G$.*\n\n4.  *The map on $X$ lands in the graph cut open at the chosen $q_e$ points, namely $\\Gamma_h$ with the resulting half-edges; it is constant on every copy of a cut at its endpoint value. All words <a href=\"#geo:relations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:relations\">[geo:relations]</a> are trivial in the fundamental group of the component $X_Q$ containing $Q$, with the original basings in $Q$.*\n\n5.  *Extend <a href=\"#geo:matrices\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:matrices\">[geo:matrices]</a> to $\\pi_1(\\Gamma)$ by $\\rho(q_e)=1$. The associated adjoint local system on $G$ restricts to $\\rho$ on the $h$ generators, to the identity on all $z_a$, and to a trivial system on each $J_e$, with matching reference frames on its two copies induced by the graph point.*\n\n*The implication uses only the asserted embedded discs. It imposes neither output-dual conditions nor an additional framing or relative homotopy condition on those discs.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-def-paired-mv",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$W_h=\\operatorname{im}\\bigl(H^1(\\Gamma_h;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)\n                 \\longrightarrow H^1(X;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)\\bigr).$$ Here the map uses the retraction of the cut-open graph onto its $h$-rose. For either degree, write $r_i^\\pm$ for restriction to the two copies of $J_i$. The map $$\\Delta(u,v)=\n  \\bigl(r_i^+u-r_i^-v,\\ r_i^-u-r_i^+v\\bigr)_{i=1}^d\n \\label{def:delta}$$ is onto in degree one, with kernel $W_h\\oplus W_h$, and is an isomorphism in degree two. Consequently, if $H^1(X;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)=W_h\\oplus U$, then $$\\Delta:U\\oplus U\\xrightarrow{\\ \\cong\\ }\n             \\bigoplus_i\\bigl(H^1(J_i;\\mathfrak l)\n                          \\oplus H^1(J_i;\\mathfrak l)\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-paired-mv",
          "exact_quote": "Let $$W_h=\\operatorname{im}\\bigl(H^1(\\Gamma_h;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)\n                 \\longrightarrow H^1(X;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)\\bigr).$$ Here the map uses the retraction of the cut-open graph onto its $h$-rose. For either degree, write $r_i^\\pm$ for restriction to the two copies of $J_i$. The map $$\\Delta(u,v)=\n  \\bigl(r_i^+u-r_i^-v,\\ r_i^-u-r_i^+v\\bigr)_{i=1}^d\n \\label{def:delta}$$ is onto in degree one, with kernel $W_h\\oplus W_h$, and is an isomorphism in degree two. Consequently, if $H^1(X;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)=W_h\\oplus U$, then $$\\Delta:U\\oplus U\\xrightarrow{\\ \\cong\\ }\n             \\bigoplus_i\\bigl(H^1(J_i;\\mathfrak l)\n                          \\oplus H^1(J_i;\\mathfrak l)\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-def-slopes",
        "exact_quote": "The ends of each cut can be designated $+$ and $-$, and torus coordinates $(\\theta_i,\\eta_i)$ of period one can be chosen, so that the image of $$H^1(J_i;\\mathbb C)\\longrightarrow H^1(\\partial J_i;\\mathbb C)$$ is the $\\theta_i$-axis. In $Q$, the $\\eta_i$-circle bounds on the positive side and the $\\theta_i$-circle bounds on the negative side. Put $$n_i=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H^1(J_i,\\partial J_i;\\mathfrak l),\n \\qquad D=\\sum_i n_i,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal Y=\\bigoplus_i(\\mathbb C^{n_i}\\oplus\\mathbb C^{n_i}).$$ Then $\\dim U=3d+D$. The joint first-slope restriction $U\\to\\mathfrak l^d$ is onto. Its kernel $U_0$ has dimension $D$. The two maps from $U_0$ to $\\mathcal Y$ given by the paired restrictions and by their interchange are injective and have complementary images.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-def-equation-chart",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a compact smooth manifold, possibly disconnected and with boundary, and let $\\rho_Z$ be a fixed $\\operatorname{SL}_2(\\mathbb C)$-local system. Choose a subspace $T\\subset H^1(Z;\\mathfrak l_{\\rho_Z})$. There is an algebraic family of edge matrices on a finite good cover, with parameters $T$, having these properties:*\n\n1.  *Its reference value is the chosen local system, its tangent is the specified inclusion of $T$, and its triangle-defect ideal $\\mathfrak r_Z$ is contained in $\\mathfrak m^2$.*\n\n2.  *Modulo $\\mathfrak r_Z$, its matrices are the transitions of an exact flat family.*\n\n3.  *For a nonzero functional $\\lambda:\\mathfrak r_Z/\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak r_Z\\to\\mathbb C$, let $\\mathfrak s_\\lambda\\subset\\mathfrak r_Z$ be the inverse image of $\\ker\\lambda$. The specified flat family over $B/\\mathfrak r_Z$, where $B=\\mathbb C[[T]]$, has no flat lift over $B/\\mathfrak s_\\lambda$. This excludes compatible general-linear lifts as well, and does not require a putative lift to remain in the parameter slice $T$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-equation-chart",

The specified flat family over $B/\\mathfrak r_Z$, where $B=\\mathbb C[[T]]$, has no flat lift over $B/\\mathfrak s_\\lambda$. This excludes compatible general-linear lifts as well, and does not require a putative lift to remain in the parameter slice $T$.",

        "exact_quote": "There are wall charts with parameters $$(a_i,y_i)\\in\\mathbb C^3\\oplus\\mathbb C^{n_i},$$ vertex parameters $(a,t)\\in\\mathbb C^{3d}\\oplus\\mathbb C^D$, a vertex defect ideal $\\mathfrak r\\subset\\mathbb C[[a,t]]$, and algebraic maps $$\\iota_i^+(a,t)=(a_i,Y_i^+(a,t)),\\qquad\n \\iota_i^-(a,t)=(0,Y_i^-(a,t)),\n \\label{def:restriction-maps}$$ with the following properties. The maps give the actual based restrictions of the flat vertex family modulo $\\mathfrak r$. The $a_i$ are algebraic group coordinates for the first-slope holonomy, centered at the identity. The two maps $$t\\longmapsto Y(0,t),\\qquad\n t\\longmapsto \\sigma Y(0,t),\n \\quad Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d,$$ have injective and complementary tangent images in $\\mathcal Y$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-def-cubic-nilpotence",
        "exact_quote": "In $B/\\mathfrak r$, where $B=\\mathbb C[[a,t]]$, let $\\mathfrak n$ be generated by all matrix entries of the end holonomies minus $1$.         "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-conn-image-bundle",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite algebraic transition-cochain chart on a compact smooth manifold $Z$, let $\\mathfrak d$ be its triangle-defect ideal. There is a smooth formal rank-two bundle and connection whose reduction modulo $\\mathfrak d$ is the specified flat family. Its fiber curvature lies in the closed extension of $\\mathfrak d$.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-conn-output",
        "exact_quote": "The algebraic maps in Proposition <a href=\"#def:restriction-charts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:restriction-charts\">17</a> and the formal functions $P_i\\in\\mathbb C[[a_i,y_i]]$ have the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$P_i\\in\\mathfrak m_i^3$, its gradient is zero modulo $\\mathfrak k_i$, and all its gradient jets have representatives in one fixed complex algebraic chart ring as in Proposition <a href=\"#conn:all-jets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conn:all-jets\">28</a>.*\n\n2.  *The two tangent images of $Y(0,t)$ and $\\sigma Y(0,t)$ are complementary $D$-planes in the $2D$-dimensional $C$-space.*\n\n3.  *For $\\Psi$ in <a href=\"#conn:psi\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conn:psi\">[conn:psi]</a>, $$\\bigl(\\partial_C\\Psi(a,Y)\\bigr)=\\mathfrak r,\\qquad\n                       \\Psi(a,Y)\\in\\mathfrak r^2 .$$*\n\n\n4.  *Choose one scalar first-slope coordinate $a_{i,1}$ for each $i$, and define $$p_i(y_i)=\\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i(0,y_i).\n     \\label{conn:p-functions}$$ For every triple of labels, including repetitions, $$a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1},\\qquad\n     p_i(Y_i^-)\\,p_j(Y_j^-)\\,p_k(Y_k^-)\n                     \\ \\in\\ \\bigl(\\partial_C\\Psi(a,Y)\\bigr).\n     \\label{conn:cubic-laws}$$*\n\n*Moreover the cube of the sum of the pulled-back wall ideals is contained in $\\mathfrak r$, as in Proposition <a href=\"#def:cubic-nilpotence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:cubic-nilpotence\">19</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-alg-arc",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a reduced complete Noetherian local $\\mathbb C$-algebra with residue field $\\mathbb C$. Every nonzero element $b$ of its maximal ideal has nonzero image under some continuous local $\\mathbb C$-algebra homomorphism $S\\longrightarrow\\mathbb C[[u]]$. Consequently, if $I\\subset B=\\mathbb C[[v_1,\\ldots,v_n]]$ is radical and $P\\in B$ satisfies $\\partial_{v_j}P\\in I$ for every $j$, then $P-P(0)\\in I$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-alg-remainder",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a commutative ring and $\\Phi(a,C)\\in(a,C)^3R[[a,C]]$, where $C$ has $N$ entries. There is an $N$-by-$N$ matrix $H(a,C,\\delta)$ over $R[[a,C,\\delta]]$, with every entry in $(a,C,\\delta)$, such that $$\\Phi(a,C+\\delta)\n =\\Phi(a,C)+(\\partial_C\\Phi)(a,C)^{\\mathsf T}\\delta\n        +\\delta^{\\mathsf T}H(a,C,\\delta)\\delta.\n \\label{alg:remainder-formula}$$ There is also a matrix $G(a,C,\\delta)$ with positive-order entries satisfying $$(\\partial_C\\Phi)(a,C+\\delta)-(\\partial_C\\Phi)(a,C)\n       =G(a,C,\\delta)\\delta.\n \\label{alg:gradient-difference}$$ Both constructions use only addition, multiplication, and integer binomial coefficients. The difference of any formal scalar function at $C+\\delta$ and $C$ similarly factors as a row times $\\delta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $R$ be a commutative ring and $\\Phi(a,C)\\in(a,C)^3R[[a,C]]$, where $C$ has $N$ entries. There is an $N$-by-$N$ matrix $H(a,C,\\delta)$ over $R[[a,C,\\delta]]$, with every entry in $(a,C,\\delta)$, such that $$\\Phi(a,C+\\delta)\n =\\Phi(a,C)+(\\partial_C\\Phi)(a,C)^{\\mathsf T}\\delta\n        +\\delta^{\\mathsf T}H(a,C,\\delta)\\delta.\n \\label{alg:remainder-formula}$$ There is also a matrix $G(a,C,\\delta)$ with positive-order entries satisfying $$(\\partial_C\\Phi)(a,C+\\delta)-(\\partial_C\\Phi)(a,C)\n       =G(a,C,\\delta)\\delta.\n \\label{alg:gradient-difference}$$ Both constructions use only addition, multiplication, and integer binomial coefficients. The difference of any formal scalar function at $C+\\delta$ and $C$ similarly factors as a row times $\\delta$.",
      "explanation": "Monomial binomial expansion gives a quadratic remainder and a positive-order gradient-difference factorization over any commutative coefficient ring. Neither construction divides by numerical factorials.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-alg-formal-data",
        "exact_quote": "The input in Definition <a href=\"#alg:input-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"alg:input-data\">31</a> yields a finitely generated $\\mathbb Z$-subalgebra $R\\subset\\mathbb C$, separated potentials $$\\begin{gathered}\n P_i\\in(a_i,y_i)^3R[[a_i,y_i]],\\qquad\n p_i(y_i)=\\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i(0,y_i),\\\\\n \\Psi(a,C)=\\sum_i\\bigl(P_i(a_i,C_i^+)-P_i(0,C_i^-)\\bigr),\n \\end{gathered}$$ and a centered formal map $\\widetilde Y(a,t)\\in(z)R[[z]]^{2D}$ with the same first-order part as the original $Y$. With $\\widetilde v=(\\partial_C\\Psi)(a,\\widetilde Y)$, there are row vectors $\\widetilde h_+^{ijk},\\widetilde h_-^{ijk}$ over $R[[z]]$ such that, for every ordered triple of labels, $$\\begin{split}\n  \\Psi(a,\\widetilde Y)&=0,\\\\\n  a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}&=\\widetilde h_+^{ijk}\\widetilde v,\\\\\n  p_i(\\widetilde Y_i^-)\n  p_j(\\widetilde Y_j^-)\n  p_k(\\widetilde Y_k^-)&=\\widetilde h_-^{ijk}\\widetilde v.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{alg:seven}$$ The determinant $\\det[\\partial_t\\widetilde Y(0,0)\\ \\sigma\\partial_t\\widetilde Y(0,0)]$ is a unit of $R$. In particular $\\widetilde Y(0,t)$ and $\\sigma\\widetilde Y(0,t)$ parametrize smooth formal submanifolds of the $C$-space with complementary tangent spaces. On both submanifolds $F(C)=\\Psi(0,C)$ vanishes identically. The same ring $R$ contains the coefficients of the potentials, the corrected map, and all the displayed membership witnesses.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-alg-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large rational prime $\\ell$, the ring $R$ in Theorem <a href=\"#alg:formal-data\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"alg:formal-data\">38</a> has a homomorphism to a finite field $\\Bbbk$ of characteristic $\\ell$. Reducing every coefficient by such a homomorphism gives separated potentials of order at least three, the identities <a href=\"#alg:seven\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:seven\">[alg:seven]</a> with their witnesses, and the two complementary zero graphs over $\\Bbbk$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frob-no-data",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Bbbk$ be a field of prime characteristic $\\ell>2$, and let $d\\geq5$. For $1\\leq i\\leq d$, choose an integer $n_i\\geq0$, a tuple $a_i=(a_{i,1},a_{i,2},a_{i,3})$ of variables, and a tuple $y_i$ of $n_i$ variables. Put $D=\\sum_i n_i$, write $a=(a_1,\\ldots,a_d)$, and let $C=(C_i^+,C_i^-)_{i=1}^d$ be $2D$ variables, with $n_i$ variables in each indicated block. There do not exist series $$P_i\\in(a_i,y_i)^3\\Bbbk[[a_i,y_i]],\\qquad\n Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d\\in\n       (a,t)\\Bbbk[[a,t]]^{2D},\\qquad t=(t_1,\\ldots,t_D),$$ having the following properties. Define $$\\Psi(a,C)=\\sum_{i=1}^d\n       \\bigl(P_i(a_i,C_i^+)-P_i(0,C_i^-)\\bigr),\\qquad\n p_i(y_i)=(\\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i)(0,y_i),\n \\label{frob:separated}$$ and let $\\sigma$ interchange the $+$ and $-$ blocks for each $i$. All indicated derivatives are taken before substitution. First, the two linear maps $$A=\\partial_tY(0,0),\\qquad \\sigma A:\n                  \\Bbbk^D\\longrightarrow\\Bbbk^{2D}$$ have complementary images and are injective, or equivalently the square matrix $[A\\ \\sigma A]$ is invertible. Second, $$\\Psi(a,Y(a,t))=0.\n \\label{frob:graph-zero}$$ Finally, for every $i,j,k$ (including repetitions), both memberships $$\\begin{split}\n a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}&\\in J_Y,\\\\\n p_i(Y_i^-(a,t))p_j(Y_j^-(a,t))p_k(Y_k^-(a,t))&\\in J_Y,\n \\qquad\n J_Y=\\bigl((\\partial_{C_\\nu}\\Psi)(a,Y(a,t))\\bigr)_{\\nu=1}^{2D}\n \\end{split}\n \\label{frob:memberships}$$ hold in $\\Bbbk[[a,t]]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-frob-normal-cycle",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $\\Gamma$ is a formal smooth $D$-dimensional graph in the $C$ variables, centered at the origin, with $F|_\\Gamma=0$. Complete a parametrization to formal coordinates $(t,u)$, with $\\Gamma$ given by $u_1=\\cdots=u_D=0$. In $\\Omega$ its normal form $$\\gamma=\\left(\\prod_{b=1}^D u_b^{\\ell-1}\\right)\n                    \\mathrm du_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge\\mathrm du_D\n \\label{frob:normal-form}$$ is annihilated by the normal ideal $(u)$ and satisfies $\\mathrm d\\gamma=\\delta\\gamma=0$. If an ambient function $g$ has a restricted expression $$g|_\\Gamma=\\sum_{\\nu=1}^{2D}h_\\nu(t)\n                               (\\partial_{C_\\nu}F)|_\\Gamma,\n \\label{frob:restricted-membership}$$ then $g\\gamma$ is a Koszul boundary. More precisely, lift $h_\\nu$ to $\\widetilde h_\\nu(C)$ by the inverse tangential coordinates and let $\\iota_\\nu$ be contraction with $\\partial/\\partial C_\\nu$. Then $$g\\gamma=\\delta\\left(\\sum_\\nu\n                         \\widetilde h_\\nu\\iota_\\nu\\gamma\\right).\n \\label{frob:primitive-general}$$ If two such graphs have complementary tangent spaces, their normal forms have nonzero residue pairing.",
        "source_id": "PM-305.1"
      },

In $\\Omega$ its normal form $$\\gamma=\\left(\\prod_{b=1}^D u_b^{\\ell-1}\\right)\n                    \\mathrm du_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge\\mathrm du_D\n \\label{frob:normal-form}$$ is annihilated by the normal ideal $(u)$ and satisfies $\\mathrm d\\gamma=\\delta\\gamma=0$. If an ambient function $g$ has a restricted expression $$g|_\\Gamma=\\sum_{\\nu=1}^{2D}h_\\nu(t)\n                               (\\partial_{C_\\nu}F)|_\\Gamma,\n \\label{frob:restricted-membership}$$ then $g\\gamma$ is a Koszul boundary. More precisely, lift $h_\\nu$ to $\\widetilde h_\\nu(C)$ by the inverse tangential coordinates and let $\\iota_\\nu$ be contraction with $\\partial/\\partial C_\\nu$. Then $$g\\gamma=\\delta\\left(\\sum_\\nu\n                         \\widetilde h_\\nu\\iota_\\nu\\gamma\\right).\n \\label{frob:primitive-general}$$ If two such graphs have complementary tangent spaces, their normal forms have nonzero residue pairing.",

In $\\Omega$ its normal form $$\\gamma=\\left(\\prod_{b=1}^D u_b^{\\ell-1}\\right)\n                    \\mathrm du_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge\\mathrm du_D\n \\label{frob:normal-form}$$ is annihilated by the normal ideal $(u)$ and satisfies $\\mathrm d\\gamma=\\delta\\gamma=0$. More precisely, lift $h_\\nu$ to $\\widetilde h_\\nu(C)$ by the inverse tangential coordinates and let $\\iota_\\nu$ be contraction with $\\partial/\\partial C_\\nu$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Equivariant intersection pairing** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The equivariant intersection pairing $\lambda$ records an intersection point with its sign and the element of $\pi$ obtained from these paths; its values lie in $\mathbb Z[\pi]$.
- **Algebraic dual spheres** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A family of sphere maps $g_j$ is algebraically dual to disc maps $f_i$ if $\lambda(f_i,g_j)=\delta_{ij}1$.
- **Geometric dual** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A geometric dual meets its corresponding disc in one transverse point and misses the other discs.
- **Reduced Wall invariant** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We use the reduced Wall invariant $$\label{eq:wall-convention} \widetilde\mu\colon\pi_2(M)\longrightarrow \mathbb Z[\pi]/\langle h-h^{-1},\,\mathbb Z\cdot1\rangle_{\mathrm{add}}.$$
- **Framed surface** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A framed surface has a trivialized real normal two-plane bundle.
- **Commutator and conjugation notation** ([source](#geo:relations)): Write $[u,v]=uvu^{-1}v^{-1}$ and $z^c=czc^{-1}$.
- **Finite conjugator word set** ([source](#geo:relations)): Take $\mathcal H$ to be all reduced words of length at most five in $h_0^{\pm1},h_+^{\pm1},h_-^{\pm1}$, including the empty word.
- **Grope body** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A *body* means one of these embedded punctured tori, excluding its caps.
- **Scalar rings and formal coefficients** ([source](#stmt-def-coefficient-convention)): An algebraic parameter chart will mean a local $\mathbb C$-algebra essentially of finite type and etale over an affine coordinate space at the designated origin. Its completion is $B=\mathbb C[[v_1,\ldots,v_N]]$, with maximal ideal $\mathfrak m$. Finite collections of algebraic germs obtained by an implicit equation with invertible Jacobian can always be placed in one such chart, after replacing it by a common etale neighborhood. For a smooth manifold $Z$, smooth forms with formal coefficients mean $$\Omega^j(Z)\widehat\otimes B =\varprojlim_k\bigl(\Omega^j(Z)\otimes_{\mathbb C} B/\mathfrak m^k\bigr).$$ For quotients of $B$, use the analogous inverse limit. The extension of a scalar ideal $I$ to this space always means its closed extension. Differential operations in the $Z$-variables preserve that extension; multiplication respects its powers. Integration over a compact manifold is defined at each finite parameter quotient and then by inverse limit.
- **Wall ideal** ([source](#def:wall-ideal)): $$U_i=-\log H_{i,1},\qquad V_i=-\log H_{i,2},\qquad \mathfrak k_i= \bigl(\mathfrak d_i,\ H_{i,1}-1,\ H_{i,2}-1\bigr). \label{def:wall-ideal}$$ Matrix entries generate the ideals in this notation. The logarithms are formal matrix logarithms; no algebraicity of their full series is asserted. They are traceless, because the matrices have determinant one and reference value $1$. The second-slope restriction has zero derivative, so $$V_i\in\mathfrak m_i^2,\qquad \mathfrak d_i\subset\mathfrak m_i^2.$$ The first slope depends exactly on $a_i$, not on $y_i$. The convention $U_i=-\log H_{i,1}$ is suited to the connection convention $d+A$, whose constant-circle holonomy is $\exp(-A(\partial_\theta))$.
- **Polarized wall function** ([source](#stmt-conn-potential-definition)): Use the global frame in Lemma <a href="#conn:wall-collar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="conn:wall-collar">21</a>, and denote the fiber connection form by $A_i$. With $\epsilon_i=\int_{\partial J_i}d\theta_i\wedge d\eta_i$, put $$\begin{aligned} \operatorname{CS}_{J_i}(A_i) &=\int_{J_i}\operatorname{tr} \left(A_i\wedge dA_i+\frac23 A_i\wedge A_i\wedge A_i\right), \notag\\ P_i(a_i,y_i) &=\operatorname{CS}_{J_i}(A_i) +\epsilon_i\operatorname{tr}(U_iV_i). \label{conn:polarized-potential} \end{aligned}$$ The differential in this integral is fiberwise. Integrals over all closed components are included with their chosen orientations.
- **Separated potential** ([source](#conn:psi)): Define the separated function and its restriction by $$\Psi(a,C)=\sum_{i=1}^d \bigl(P_i(a_i,C_i^+)-P_i(0,C_i^-)\bigr), \qquad C=(C_i^+,C_i^-)_{i=1}^d\in\mathcal Y , \label{conn:psi}$$
- **Algebraic input package** ([source](#stmt-alg-input-data)): Fix finitely many labels $1\leq i\leq d$, integers $n_i\geq0$, and $D=\sum_i n_i$. Write $$a_i=(a_{i,1},a_{i,2},a_{i,3}),\qquad y_i\in\mathbb C^{n_i},\qquad C=(C_i^+,C_i^-)_{i=1}^d\in\mathbb C^{2D},\qquad z=(a,t),\quad t\in\mathbb C^D.$$ An algebraic germ in specified coordinates means a regular germ at the specified complex point of an étale neighborhood of their affine coordinate space, expanded in those coordinates. Finitely many such germs can be placed in a common neighborhood. The following are the input data established by the preceding geometric and connection constructions. 1. For each $i$ there is a local étale complex ring $A_i$ in coordinates $(a_i,y_i)$, with completion $B_i=\mathbb C[[a_i,y_i]]$, maximal ideal $\mathfrak m_i$, and an ideal $\mathfrak k_i\subset\mathfrak m_i$. The formal potential $P_i^{\mathrm{o}}\in\mathfrak m_i^3B_i$ satisfies, for every wall coordinate $x$, $$\partial_xP_i^{\mathrm{o}}\in\mathfrak k_iB_i,\qquad \partial_xP_i^{\mathrm{o}}\in A_i+\mathfrak k_i^bB_i \quad(b\geq1).$$ The second assertion means that a representative in the *same* ring $A_i$ exists at every indicated ideal order; it does not assert that the full gradient is algebraic. 2. There are centered algebraic germs $Y_i^\pm(z)$ and an algebraic ideal $\mathfrak r$ in a local étale chart in $z$. All ideals in the following identities are extended to $B_X=\mathbb C[[z]]$, whose maximal ideal is $\mathfrak m$. Put $$\phi_i^+(z)=(a_i,Y_i^+(z)),\qquad \phi_i^-(z)=(0,Y_i^-(z)),\qquad L_i^\pm=\phi_i^{\pm *}(\mathfrak k_i)B_X.$$ Then $L_i^\pm\subset\mathfrak m$ and $$(L_i^\pm)^3\subset\mathfrak r. \label{alg:cube-input}$$ 3. With $Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d$, define $$\Psi^{\mathrm{o}}(a,C) =\sum_{i=1}^d\bigl(P_i^{\mathrm{o}}(a_i,C_i^+) -P_i^{\mathrm{o}}(0,C_i^-)\bigr), \qquad p_i^{\mathrm{o}}(y_i)=\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i^{\mathrm{o}}(0,y_i).$$ Differentiation with respect to $C$ is performed before substitution. The following identities hold in $B_X$: $$\bigl((\partial_C\Psi^{\mathrm{o}})(a,Y)\bigr)=\mathfrak r, \qquad \Psi^{\mathrm{o}}(a,Y)\in\mathfrak r^2, \label{alg:value-gradient-input}$$ and, for every ordered triple $(i,j,k)$, with repetitions allowed, $$a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}\in\mathfrak r,\qquad p_i^{\mathrm{o}}(Y_i^-) p_j^{\mathrm{o}}(Y_j^-) p_k^{\mathrm{o}}(Y_k^-)\in\mathfrak r. \label{alg:triple-input}$$ Here the original triple memberships and <a href="#alg:cube-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:cube-input">[alg:cube-input]</a> are consequences of the end-deviation cube law on the original restriction graph. 4. Let $\sigma$ exchange $C_i^+$ and $C_i^-$ for every $i$, and set $J=\partial_tY(0,0)$, a $2D$-by-$D$ matrix. The two tangent images are complementary: $$\Delta=\det[\,J\ \ \sigma J\,]\ne0. \label{alg:tangent-input}$$
- **Coordinate Frobenius quotient and forms** ([source](#frob:quotient)): Fix an ordering $C_1,\ldots,C_{2D}$, in block order $1^+,1^-,2^+,2^-,\ldots,d^+,d^-$. Define $$T=\Bbbk[[C]]/(C_1^\ell,\ldots,C_{2D}^\ell),\qquad \Omega^q=T\otimes_{\Bbbk} \bigwedge^q\langle\mathrm dC_1,\ldots,\mathrm dC_{2D}\rangle. \label{frob:quotient}$$
- **Koszul differential and cohomology** ([source](#frob:forms-subsection)): For $F\in\Bbbk[[C]]$ put $$\delta=\mathrm dF\wedge{},\qquad H=H^*(\Omega,\delta).$$
- **Coordinate residue** ([source](#frob:residue-exact)): Define $\operatorname{Res}_C$ on $\Omega$ by taking, in top form degree, the coefficient of $C_1^{\ell-1}\cdots C_{2D}^{\ell-1}$ in the coefficient of $\mathrm dC_1\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm dC_{2D}$; it is zero in other degrees.

The logarithms are formal matrix logarithms; no algebraicity of their full series is asserted. They are traceless, because the matrices have determinant one and reference value $1$. The second-slope restriction has zero derivative, so $$V_i\\in\\mathfrak m_i^2,\\qquad \\mathfrak d_i\\subset\\mathfrak m_i^2.$$ The first slope depends exactly on $a_i$, not on $y_i$. The convention $U_i=-\\log H_{i,1}$ is suited to the connection convention $d+A$, whose constant-circle holonomy is $\\exp(-A(\\partial_\\theta))$.",
      "interpretation": "Matrix entries of the wall defects and both holonomy deviations generate the scalar ideal. The formal logarithms use the connection sign convention.",

      "name": "Wall ideal",
      "scope": "Each wall chart."

          "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many labels $1\\leq i\\leq d$, integers $n_i\\geq0$, and $D=\\sum_i n_i$. Write $$a_i=(a_{i,1},a_{i,2},a_{i,3}),\\qquad\n y_i\\in\\mathbb C^{n_i},\\qquad\n C=(C_i^+,C_i^-)_{i=1}^d\\in\\mathbb C^{2D},\\qquad\n z=(a,t),\\quad t\\in\\mathbb C^D.$$ An algebraic germ in specified coordinates means a regular germ at the specified complex point of an étale neighborhood of their affine coordinate space, expanded in those coordinates. Finitely many such germs can be placed in a common neighborhood. The following are the input data established by the preceding geometric and connection constructions.\n\n1.  For each $i$ there is a local étale complex ring $A_i$ in coordinates $(a_i,y_i)$, with completion $B_i=\\mathbb C[[a_i,y_i]]$, maximal ideal $\\mathfrak m_i$, and an ideal $\\mathfrak k_i\\subset\\mathfrak m_i$. The formal potential $P_i^{\\mathrm{o}}\\in\\mathfrak m_i^3B_i$ satisfies, for every wall coordinate $x$, $$\\partial_xP_i^{\\mathrm{o}}\\in\\mathfrak k_iB_i,\\qquad\n     \\partial_xP_i^{\\mathrm{o}}\\in A_i+\\mathfrak k_i^bB_i\n     \\quad(b\\geq1).$$ The second assertion means that a representative in the *same* ring $A_i$ exists at every indicated ideal order; it does not assert that the full gradient is algebraic.\n\n\n2.  There are centered algebraic germs $Y_i^\\pm(z)$ and an algebraic ideal $\\mathfrak r$ in a local étale chart in $z$. All ideals in the following identities are extended to $B_X=\\mathbb C[[z]]$, whose maximal ideal is $\\mathfrak m$. Put $$\\phi_i^+(z)=(a_i,Y_i^+(z)),\\qquad\n     \\phi_i^-(z)=(0,Y_i^-(z)),\\qquad\n     L_i^\\pm=\\phi_i^{\\pm *}(\\mathfrak k_i)B_X.$$ Then $L_i^\\pm\\subset\\mathfrak m$ and $$(L_i^\\pm)^3\\subset\\mathfrak r.\n     \\label{alg:cube-input}$$\n\n\n3.  With $Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d$, define $$\\Psi^{\\mathrm{o}}(a,C)\n     =\\sum_{i=1}^d\\bigl(P_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(a_i,C_i^+)\n                        -P_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(0,C_i^-)\\bigr),\n     \\qquad\n     p_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(y_i)=\\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(0,y_i).$$ Differentiation with respect to $C$ is performed before substitution. The following identities hold in $B_X$: $$\\bigl((\\partial_C\\Psi^{\\mathrm{o}})(a,Y)\\bigr)=\\mathfrak r,\n     \\qquad \\Psi^{\\mathrm{o}}(a,Y)\\in\\mathfrak r^2,\n     \\label{alg:value-gradient-input}$$ and, for every ordered triple $(i,j,k)$, with repetitions allowed, $$a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}\\in\\mathfrak r,\\qquad\n     p_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(Y_i^-)\n     p_j^{\\mathrm{o}}(Y_j^-)\n     p_k^{\\mathrm{o}}(Y_k^-)\\in\\mathfrak r.\n     \\label{alg:triple-input}$$ Here the original triple memberships and <a href=\"#alg:cube-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:cube-input\">[alg:cube-input]</a> are consequences of the end-deviation cube law on the original restriction graph.\n\n4.  Let $\\sigma$ exchange $C_i^+$ and $C_i^-$ for every $i$, and set $J=\\partial_tY(0,0)$, a $2D$-by-$D$ matrix. The two tangent images are complementary: $$\\Delta=\\det[\\,J\\ \\ \\sigma J\\,]\\ne0.\n     \\label{alg:tangent-input}$$",

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-wall-label-lower-bound | Number of wall labels | ≥ | $5$ | The chosen integer indexing wall pairs. |
| q-ambient-handle-count | Handle count of the ambient handlebody | = | $3+2d$ | The handle count depends on the chosen wall-label integer. |
| q-finite-relation-count | Number of relations and input discs | = | $(2d\,\|\mathcal H\|)^3$ | The count depends on the end labels and the fixed finite conjugator set. |
| q-adjoint-eigenvalues | Eigenvalues of the diagonal reference adjoint operator | = | $4,1,1/4$ | The fixed reference representation. |
| q-vertex-complement-dimension | Dimension of the complementary vertex tangent space | = | $3d+D$ | The wall labels and sum of internal wall dimensions. |
| q-wall-cohomology-dimension | Dimension of wall degree-one cohomology | = | $3+n_i$ | An individual wall, including its closed components. |
| q-end-ideal-cube | Cube of the end-deviation ideal | = | $0$ | All end holonomy matrix deviations together. |
| q-potential-minimal-order | Maximal-ideal order of the polarized wall potential | ≥ | $3$ | Each polarized wall potential. |
| q-replacement-ideal-order | Wall-ideal order of the algebraic replacement error | ≥ | $9$ | The original formal potential and its algebraic replacement. |
| q-replacement-value-error | Defect-ideal order of each pulled-back replacement value error | ≥ | $3$ | Either pullback of each wall replacement error. |
| q-frobenius-characteristic-regime | Prime characteristic of the obstruction field | > | $2$ | The field characteristic in the formal nonexistence theorem. |
| q-tensor-kernel-factor-threshold | Number of individual kernel factors in the joint-kernel sum | ≥ | $d-2$ | The subsets indexing the stated sum of tensor subspaces. |

For each $i$ there is a local étale complex ring $A_i$ in coordinates $(a_i,y_i)$, with completion $B_i=\\mathbb C[[a_i,y_i]]$, maximal ideal $\\mathfrak m_i$, and an ideal $\\mathfrak k_i\\subset\\mathfrak m_i$. There are centered algebraic germs $Y_i^\\pm(z)$ and an algebraic ideal $\\mathfrak r$ in a local étale chart in $z$. All ideals in the following identities are extended to $B_X=\\mathbb C[[z]]$, whose maximal ideal is $\\mathfrak m$. With $Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d$, define $$\\Psi^{\\mathrm{o}}(a,C)\n     =\\sum_{i=1}^d\\bigl(P_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(a_i,C_i^+)\n                        -P_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(0,C_i^-)\\bigr),\n     \\qquad\n     p_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(y_i)=\\partial_{a_{i,1}}P_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(0,y_i).$$ Differentiation with respect to $C$ is performed before substitution. The following identities hold in $B_X$: $$\\bigl((\\partial_C\\Psi^{\\mathrm{o}})(a,Y)\\bigr)=\\mathfrak r,\n     \\qquad \\Psi^{\\mathrm{o}}(a,Y)\\in\\mathfrak r^2,\n     \\label{alg:value-gradient-input}$$ and, for every ordered triple $(i,j,k)$, with repetitions allowed, $$a_{i,1}a_{j,1}a_{k,1}\\in\\mathfrak r,\\qquad\n     p_i^{\\mathrm{o}}(Y_i^-)\n     p_j^{\\mathrm{o}}(Y_j^-)\n     p_k^{\\mathrm{o}}(Y_k^-)\\in\\mathfrak r.\n     \\label{alg:triple-input}$$ Here the original triple memberships and <a href=\"#alg:cube-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:cube-input\">[alg:cube-input]</a> are consequences of the end-deviation cube law on the original restriction graph.\n\n4.  Let $\\sigma$ exchange $C_i^+$ and $C_i^-$ for every $i$, and set $J=\\partial_tY(0,0)$, a $2D$-by-$D$ matrix. The two tangent images are complementary: $$\\Delta=\\det[\\,J\\ \\ \\sigma J\\,]\\ne0.\n     \\label{alg:tangent-input}$$",
