<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-graph-model** [claim](#stmt-geo-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-graph): Van Kampen and compatible homotopy-pushout retractions. -> Free exterior group and boundary-controlled graph homotopy equivalence.
- **step-ribbon-framings** [claim](#stmt-geo-framing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-framing): Finger isotopy, oriented ribbon thickening and ordered normal-quotient calculation. -> Commutator ribbon with independently adjustable attachment framings.
- **step-capped-stages** [claim](#stmt-geo-stages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-stages): Sequential collar-level construction and upper-stage compression. -> Disjoint bodies and collars with framed embedded contracting discs.
- **step-dual-candidates** [claim](#stmt-geo-dual-candidates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-dual-candidates): Product-torus compression and homological normal Euler-number calculation. -> Individually embedded framed spheres with vanishing diagonal invariants.
- **step-exterior-labels** [claim](#stmt-geo-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-labels): Cap-parallel collapse and nullhomotopy in the retained upper-stage carrier. -> Equal exterior group labels and opposite intersection signs.
- **step-input-invariants** [claim](#stmt-geo-input-invariants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-input-invariants): Orient and rebase the remaining diagonal intersection. -> Exact algebraic duality and all required boundary conditions.
- **step-protected-cuts** [claim](#stmt-geo-topological-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-topological-cuts): Neighborhood extension of the prescribed homotopy, followed by relative approximation and transversality away from protected images. -> Smooth cuts with their fixed torus boundaries.
- **step-geometric-package** [claim](#stmt-geo-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-construction): Cut the graph levels and retain the based relations carried by the protected pieces. -> Conditional cut manifold, wall data, based relations and matching local systems.
- **step-paired-restrictions** [claim](#stmt-def-paired-mv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-paired-mv): Mayer–Vietoris for the graph-derived cover. -> Paired restriction surjectivity and degree-two isomorphism.
- **step-slopes** [claim](#stmt-def-slopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-slopes): Boundary duality and the longitude–meridian interchange. -> First-slope coordinates and complementary internal tangent images.
- **step-equation-charts** [claim](#stmt-def-equation-chart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-equation-chart): Finite transition cochains, algebraic implicit elimination and small-extension obstruction detection. -> Algebraic charts retaining the entire minimal defect layer.
- **step-restriction-charts** [claim](#stmt-def-restriction-charts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-restriction-charts): Based tree frames and algebraic restriction words. -> Algebraic wall restrictions with complementary tangent graphs.
- **step-adjoint-spanning** [claim](#stmt-geo-spanning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-spanning): Spectral projections and unipotent adjoint matrix units. -> Spanning of the Lie-algebra endomorphism space.
- **step-cubic-holonomy** [claim](#stmt-def-cubic-nilpotence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-cubic-nilpotence): Leading commutator expansion, independent conjugator linear combinations and Nakayama's lemma. -> Cube-zero end-deviation ideal and pulled-back wall-ideal cube containment.
- **step-image-bundle** [claim](#stmt-conn-image-bundle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-image-bundle): Formal idempotent correction and partition-of-unity blending. -> Genuine formal bundle and connection preserving the flat reduction.
- **step-wall-collars** [claim](#stmt-conn-wall-collar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-wall-collar): Logarithmic holonomy frames, averaged frame lifts and collar interpolation. -> Prescribed collar forms and extending global wall frames.
- **step-polarized-variation** [claim](#stmt-conn-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-variation): Differentiate the Chern–Simons integrand and cancel the boundary term with polarization. -> Gradient membership in the wall ideal and cubic potential order.
- **step-vertex-connection** [claim](#stmt-conn-vertex-connection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-vertex-connection): Lift the graph-derived reference frame and correct the determinant connection. -> Global connection with traceless defect-valued curvature.
- **step-derivative-ideal** [claim](#stmt-conn-derivative-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-derivative-ideal): Small-extension curvature obstruction, relative wall duality, paired degree-two detection and Nakayama's lemma. -> Equality of internal derivative and full vertex defect ideals.
- **step-closed-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-conn-closed-comparisons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-closed-comparisons): Closed-manifold variation along a connection perturbation and separately along a gauge change. -> Square-ideal connection difference and parameter-constant gauge difference.
- **step-square-value** [claim](#stmt-conn-value-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-value-ideal): Pure collar modification, flat exterior extension, boundary comparison and Stokes. -> Potential value in the square of the vertex defect ideal.
- **step-fixed-ring-jets** [claim](#stmt-conn-all-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-all-jets): Frame-independent mixed-curvature variation and finite ideal-order expansions of local expressions. -> All finite gradient jets represented in the same chart ring.
- **step-deformation-output** [claim](#stmt-conn-output) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conn-output): Evaluate the negative-wall slope variation and use the end-deviation cube law. -> The full algebraic input with both cubic membership families.
- **step-arc-detection** [claim](#stmt-alg-arc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-arc): Pass to a domain and a curve quotient, normalize, and apply the formal chain rule. -> Detecting formal arc and constancy modulo a radical gradient ideal.
- **step-primitive-replacement** [claim](#stmt-alg-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-replacement): Taylor-shifted finite modules, faithful-flat image descent and differential tests at all primary components. -> Algebraic primitive approximation at every requested ideal power.
- **step-membership-preservation** [claim](#stmt-alg-replacement-preserves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-replacement-preserves): High-order replacement estimates, Nakayama's lemma and faithful-flat contraction of membership ideals. -> Algebraic potentials and algebraic value and cubic membership witnesses.
- **step-coefficient-ring** [claim](#stmt-alg-finite-ring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-finite-ring): Finite étale presentations and degree recursion using inverse initial Jacobians. -> A common finitely generated integer coefficient ring.
- **step-remainder-factorizations** [claim](#stmt-alg-remainder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-remainder): Coefficientwise binomial expansion with fixed factor allocation. -> Denominator-free value and gradient remainder factorizations.
- **step-corrected-graph** [claim](#stmt-alg-formal-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-formal-data): Contracting matrix recursion and invertible gradient-generator transport. -> Exact zero graph, transported cubic laws and unchanged complementary tangents.
- **step-specialization** [claim](#stmt-alg-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-specialization): Number-field specialization and reduction away from finitely many denominator primes. -> Prime-characteristic formal data preserving all identities and witnesses.
- **step-normal-cycles** [claim](#stmt-frob-normal-cycle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frob-normal-cycle): Coordinate Frobenius-ideal preservation, normal-volume annihilation and contraction primitives. -> Closed normal cycles, ambient boundaries and nonzero complementary residue pairing.
- **step-chain-retraction** [claim](#stmt-frob-retraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frob-retraction): Choose a graded kernel complement and build its contractible differential pair. -> Chain idempotent with an explicit homotopy and the specified kernel restriction.
- **step-tensor-kernels** [claim](#stmt-frob-tensor-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frob-tensor-kernel): Split factor domains into kernels and complements and intersect the resulting coordinate direct sums. -> Joint-kernel decomposition with enough individual kernel slots.
- **step-frobenius-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-frob-no-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frob-tensor-kernel-2): Select a tensor preserving the residue pairing, lift all mixed parameter terms, and pair a moving normal cycle with an explicit ambient primitive. -> A unit pairing forces a nonzero parameter product to vanish, excluding the formal data.
- **step-main-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-frob-tensor-kernel-3): Construct the algebraic input from a hypothetical boundary filling, correct and specialize it, then apply formal-data nonexistence. -> Nonexistence of every disjoint locally flat filling of the prescribed boundary circles.
- **step-nongood-free-group** [claim](#stmt-cor-nongood) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-nongood): Finite generation, free subgroup normal forms and subgroup–quotient closure of goodness. -> Nongoodness of the nonabelian free group and every group containing it.
- **step-surgery-conjunction** [claim](#stmt-cor-surgery-conjunction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-surgery-conjunction): Apply the cited implication from the unrestricted assertion conjunction to disc embedding. -> Failure of the conjunction.

          "exact_quote": "Given two disjoint oriented framed knots in the interior of $Q$ and a relative path class joining them, one can construct a punctured torus in a level of a collar $Q\\times[0,\\epsilon)$ whose two basis curves resolve normally to these knots. Its oriented boundary represents their commutator, with the change of basing given by the chosen path. The two band framings can be varied by arbitrary integers independently. The resolved knots admit disjoint rising annuli to the given framed knots, and a unit band twist changes the relative oriented normal framing of its annulus by a unit integer.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-ribbon-framings",
      "inputs": [
        "Framed knots",
        "Relative joining path"
      ],
      "method": "Finger isotopy, oriented ribbon thickening and ordered normal-quotient calculation.",
      "output": "Commutator ribbon with independently adjustable attachment framings.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-framing"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-geo-stages",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-ribbon-framings"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-stages",
          "exact_quote": "For every $r\\in\\mathcal R$ there is a punctured torus $B_r$ properly embedded in $V$, with boundary a knot in $\\operatorname{int}Q$. The $B_r$ are mutually disjoint. They have intersecting basis curves $\\alpha_r,\\beta_r$ and the following attachments in $G$:*\n\n1.  *A shallow cap at $\\alpha_r$ ends in a separate parallel copy of the core disc of end $a$.*\n\n2.  *A further punctured torus $S_r$ is attached at $\\beta_r$. Its basis curves $x_r,y_r$ have caps of end types $b,c$, respectively. Its boundary, with the prescribed basing, represents $[z_b^{c_2},z_c^{c_3}]$, and $\\partial B_r$ represents $R_r$.*\n\n3.  *Compressing $S_r$ along $x_r$ with two framed parallels of its cap produces an embedded disc $D_r$. Intersections of $D_r$ with other objects are allowed. The extension of $D_r$ towards $\\beta_r$ has a framing that extends the angular normal-circle vector required to compress $\\beta_r\\times S^1$.*\n\n*Except at the intended attachments, every body and attaching collar is disjoint from every other such piece. All other intersections are transverse crossings of opposite end cap sheets in the product charts <a href=\"#geo:product-sheets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:product-sheets\">[geo:product-sheets]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-capped-stages",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite independently conjugated relation family",
        "Framed ribbon operation"
      ],
      "method": "Sequential collar-level construction and upper-stage compression.",
      "output": "Disjoint bodies and collars with framed embedded contracting discs.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-stages"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-geo-dual-candidates",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-capped-stages"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-dual-candidates",
          "exact_quote": "Each $g_r$ is individually embedded and has trivial normal bundle. It follows that $$\\lambda(g_r,g_r)=0,\\qquad \\widetilde\\mu(g_r)=0.$$ The compression leaves the single product-torus intersection with $f_r$ unchanged. Every other intersection involving an $f_r$ and a $g_s$, or two distinct spheres, occurs on the cap sheets in <a href=\"#geo:product-sheets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:product-sheets\">[geo:product-sheets]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-dual-candidates",
      "inputs": [
        "Exterior of base tubes",
        "Contracting-disc framing"
      ],
      "method": "Product-torus compression and homological normal Euler-number calculation.",
      "output": "Individually embedded framed spheres with vanishing diagonal invariants.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-dual-candidates"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-geo-labels",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-dual-candidates"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-labels",
          "exact_quote": "Within one contracting-disc copy on a sphere, pair the two oppositely oriented $x$-cap sheets at their crossings with any fixed opposite-type sheet. The paired intersections have equal Wall labels in $\\pi_1(M)$ and opposite signs. All cap-sheet contributions to $\\lambda(f_r,g_s)$ and to $\\lambda(g_r,g_s)$ for $r\\ne s$ therefore cancel.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-exterior-labels",
      "inputs": [
        "Paired cap crossings",
        "Unused upper caps"
      ],
      "method": "Cap-parallel collapse and nullhomotopy in the retained upper-stage carrier.",
      "output": "Equal exterior group labels and opposite intersection signs.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-labels"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-geo-input-invariants",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-dual-candidates",
        "step-exterior-labels"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-input-invariants",
          "exact_quote": "There are orientations and whiskers for the discs and spheres such that, for all $r,s\\in\\mathcal R$ and $R=\\mathbb Z[\\pi_1(M)]$, $$\\label{geo:invariants}\n \\lambda(f_r,g_s)=\\delta_{rs}\\,1\\in R,\n \\qquad \\lambda(g_r,g_s)=0\\in R,\n \\qquad \\widetilde\\mu(g_r)=0.$$ In particular the normal second Stiefel–Whitney number of every $g_r$ is zero. These data satisfy all algebraic and boundary hypotheses of the unrestricted disc-embedding assertion.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-input-invariants",
      "inputs": [
        "Diagonal vanishing",
        "Off-diagonal cap cancellation"
      ],
      "method": "Orient and rebase the remaining diagonal intersection.",
      "output": "Exact algebraic duality and all required boundary conditions.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-input-invariants"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-geo-topological-cuts",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-graph-model",
        "step-input-invariants"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-topological-cuts",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose there are pairwise disjoint proper locally flat embeddings $\\overline f_r:D^2\\longrightarrow M$ with $\\overline f_r|_{S^1}=f_r|_{S^1}$. Then $p_0$ is homotopic, relative to $\\partial G$, to a map $p:G\\longrightarrow\\Gamma$ with the following properties. The bases, the short return annuli in their deleted tubes, and the disc images lie in $p^{-1}(\\Gamma_h)$. For one interior point of each $q_e$ edge, the inverse image $J_e$ is a compact smooth oriented three-manifold with exactly one boundary torus. Additional closed components of $J_e$ are allowed.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-protected-cuts",
      "inputs": [
        "Hypothetical disjoint locally flat fillings",
        "Boundary-controlled graph map"
      ],
      "method": "Neighborhood extension of the prescribed homotopy, followed by relative approximation and transversality away from protected images.",
      "output": "Smooth cuts with their fixed torus boundaries.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-topological-cuts"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-geo-construction",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-protected-cuts"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-construction",
          "exact_quote": "For every $d\\geq5$, with the finite word set $\\mathcal H$ defined above, the preceding construction produces a compact connected oriented smooth four-manifold $M$ with nonempty boundary, a finite family $F=(f_r)_{r\\in\\mathcal R}$ of smooth proper disc maps with disjoint embedded boundary, and individually embedded framed sphere maps $(g_r)_{r\\in\\mathcal R}$ satisfying <a href=\"#geo:invariants\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:invariants\">[geo:invariants]</a>.*\n\n*If the circles $\\partial F$ bound pairwise disjoint proper locally flat embedded discs in $M$, the following data exist:*\n\n1.  *The compact oriented smooth four-manifold $G$ has a homotopy equivalence $p:G\\longrightarrow\\Gamma$, where $\\Gamma$ is the rose on $h_0,h_+,h_-,q_1,\\ldots,q_d$. The boundary is $Q\\cup\\bigcup_e C_e$, with the free group and peripheral coordinates of Lemma <a href=\"#geo:graph\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:graph\">4</a> and the longitude–meridian interchange <a href=\"#geo:hopf-collar\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:hopf-collar\">[geo:hopf-collar]</a>.*\n\n2.  *There are disjoint cooriented smooth cuts $J_1,\\ldots,J_d$, each a compact oriented three-manifold with one boundary torus and possibly additional closed components. Their boundary tori are the fixed product cuts in the $C_e$.*\n\n3.  *Cutting $G$ along all $J_e$ gives a compact oriented smooth four-manifold $X$, possibly disconnected. With half collars absorbed into $Q$, its boundary is obtained by gluing $Q$ to the two oppositely oriented copies of each $J_e$ along their boundary tori. Every torus-bearing wall component has just that one torus; closed wall components remain separate. Regluing the paired cuts recovers $G$.*\n\n4.  *The map on $X$ lands in the graph cut open at the chosen $q_e$ points, namely $\\Gamma_h$ with the resulting half-edges; it is constant on every copy of a cut at its endpoint value. All words <a href=\"#geo:relations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:relations\">[geo:relations]</a> are trivial in the fundamental group of the component $X_Q$ containing $Q$, with the original basings in $Q$.*\n\n5.  *Extend <a href=\"#geo:matrices\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:matrices\">[geo:matrices]</a> to $\\pi_1(\\Gamma)$ by $\\rho(q_e)=1$. The associated adjoint local system on $G$ restricts to $\\rho$ on the $h$ generators, to the identity on all $z_a$, and to a trivial system on each $J_e$, with matching reference frames on its two copies induced by the graph point.*\n\n*The implication uses only the asserted embedded discs. It imposes neither output-dual conditions nor an additional framing or relative homotopy condition on those discs.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-geometric-package",
      "inputs": [
        "Smooth cuts",
        "Input discs and algebraic duals"
      ],
      "method": "Cut the graph levels and retain the based relations carried by the protected pieces.",
      "output": "Conditional cut manifold, wall data, based relations and matching local systems.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-construction"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-def-paired-mv",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-geometric-package"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-paired-mv",
          "exact_quote": "Let $$W_h=\\operatorname{im}\\bigl(H^1(\\Gamma_h;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)\n                 \\longrightarrow H^1(X;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)\\bigr).$$ Here the map uses the retraction of the cut-open graph onto its $h$-rose. For either degree, write $r_i^\\pm$ for restriction to the two copies of $J_i$. The map $$\\Delta(u,v)=\n  \\bigl(r_i^+u-r_i^-v,\\ r_i^-u-r_i^+v\\bigr)_{i=1}^d\n \\label{def:delta}$$ is onto in degree one, with kernel $W_h\\oplus W_h$, and is an isomorphism in degree two. Consequently, if $H^1(X;\\mathfrak l_\\rho)=W_h\\oplus U$, then $$\\Delta:U\\oplus U\\xrightarrow{\\ \\cong\\ }\n             \\bigoplus_i\\bigl(H^1(J_i;\\mathfrak l)\n                          \\oplus H^1(J_i;\\mathfrak l)\\bigr).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-paired-restrictions",
      "inputs": [
        "Cut manifold",
        "Adjoint local system"
      ],
      "method": "Mayer–Vietoris for the graph-derived cover.",
      "output": "Paired restriction surjectivity and degree-two isomorphism.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-def-paired-mv"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-def-slopes",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-paired-restrictions"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-slopes",
          "exact_quote": "The ends of each cut can be designated $+$ and $-$, and torus coordinates $(\\theta_i,\\eta_i)$ of period one can be chosen, so that the image of $$H^1(J_i;\\mathbb C)\\longrightarrow H^1(\\partial J_i;\\mathbb C)$$ is the $\\theta_i$-axis. In $Q$, the $\\eta_i$-circle bounds on the positive side and the $\\theta_i$-circle bounds on the negative side. Put $$n_i=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H^1(J_i,\\partial J_i;\\mathfrak l),\n \\qquad D=\\sum_i n_i,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal Y=\\bigoplus_i(\\mathbb C^{n_i}\\oplus\\mathbb C^{n_i}).$$ Then $\\dim U=3d+D$. The joint first-slope restriction $U\\to\\mathfrak l^d$ is onto. Its kernel $U_0$ has dimension $D$. The two maps from $U_0$ to $\\mathcal Y$ given by the paired restrictions and by their interchange are injective and have complementary images.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-slopes",
      "inputs": [
        "Paired restrictions",
        "Peripheral slope constraints"
      ],
      "method": "Boundary duality and the longitude–meridian interchange.",
      "output": "First-slope coordinates and complementary internal tangent images.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-def-slopes"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-def-equation-chart",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-geometric-package"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-equation-chart",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a compact smooth manifold, possibly disconnected and with boundary, and let $\\rho_Z$ be a fixed $\\operatorname{SL}_2(\\mathbb C)$-local system. Choose a subspace $T\\subset H^1(Z;\\mathfrak l_{\\rho_Z})$. There is an algebraic family of edge matrices on a finite good cover, with parameters $T$, having these properties:*\n\n1.  *Its reference value is the chosen local system, its tangent is the specified inclusion of $T$, and its triangle-defect ideal $\\mathfrak r_Z$ is contained in $\\mathfrak m^2$.*\n\n2.  *Modulo $\\mathfrak r_Z$, its matrices are the transitions of an exact flat family.*\n\n3.  *For a nonzero functional $\\lambda:\\mathfrak r_Z/\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak r_Z\\to\\mathbb C$, let $\\mathfrak s_\\lambda\\subset\\mathfrak r_Z$ be the inverse image of $\\ker\\lambda$. The specified flat family over $B/\\mathfrak r_Z$, where $B=\\mathbb C[[T]]$, has no flat lift over $B/\\mathfrak s_\\lambda$. This excludes compatible general-linear lifts as well, and does not require a putative lift to remain in the parameter slice $T$.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-equation-charts",
      "inputs": [
        "Compact smooth manifolds",
        "Chosen tangent subspaces"
      ],
      "method": "Finite transition cochains, algebraic implicit elimination and small-extension obstruction detection.",
      "output": "Algebraic charts retaining the entire minimal defect layer.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-def-equation-chart"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-def-restriction-charts",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-equation-charts",
        "step-slopes"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-restriction-charts",
          "exact_quote": "There are wall charts with parameters $$(a_i,y_i)\\in\\mathbb C^3\\oplus\\mathbb C^{n_i},$$ vertex parameters $(a,t)\\in\\mathbb C^{3d}\\oplus\\mathbb C^D$, a vertex defect ideal $\\mathfrak r\\subset\\mathbb C[[a,t]]$, and algebraic maps $$\\iota_i^+(a,t)=(a_i,Y_i^+(a,t)),\\qquad\n \\iota_i^-(a,t)=(0,Y_i^-(a,t)),\n \\label{def:restriction-maps}$$ with the following properties. The maps give the actual based restrictions of the flat vertex family modulo $\\mathfrak r$. The $a_i$ are algebraic group coordinates for the first-slope holonomy, centered at the identity. The two maps $$t\\longmapsto Y(0,t),\\qquad\n t\\longmapsto \\sigma Y(0,t),\n \\quad Y=(Y_i^+,Y_i^-)_{i=1}^d,$$ have injective and complementary tangent images in $\\mathcal Y$.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-restriction-charts",
      "inputs": [
        "Equation charts",

      ],
      "method": "Based tree frames and algebraic restriction words.",
      "output": "Algebraic wall restrictions with complementary tangent graphs.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-def-restriction-charts"
    },
    {

      ],
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-geo-spanning",
          "exact_quote": "For the word set $\\mathcal H$ fixed in Section <a href=\"#sec:geometry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:geometry\">2</a> and the representation in <a href=\"#geo:matrices\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geo:matrices\">[geo:matrices]</a>, one has $$\\operatorname{span}_{\\mathbb C}\n \\{\\operatorname{Ad}\\rho(c):c\\in\\mathcal H\\}\n       =\\operatorname{End}_{\\mathbb C}(\\mathfrak l).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"

          "anchor": "stmt-def-cubic-nilpotence",
          "exact_quote": "In $B/\\mathfrak r$, where $B=\\mathbb C[[a,t]]$, let $\\mathfrak n$ be generated by all matrix entries of the end holonomies minus $1$. Then $$\\mathfrak n^3=0.\n \\label{def:n-cube}$$ In particular, for either restriction map in <a href=\"#def:restriction-maps\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"def:restriction-maps\">[def:restriction-maps]</a>, $$(\\iota_i^\\pm)^*(\\mathfrak k_i)^3\\subset\\mathfrak r .\n \\label{def:k-cube}$$ The same conclusion holds for the cube of the sum of all these pulled-back ideals.",

          "exact_quote": "For a finite algebraic transition-cochain chart on a compact smooth manifold $Z$, let $\\mathfrak d$ be its triangle-defect ideal. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-joint-tensor-kernels**: The joint kernel of the products acting in distinct triples of tensor slots equals the sum of tensor subspaces with kernel factors in sufficiently many slots. Additional passive factors do not change this identity.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-algebraic-duals-imply-fillings**: Infer disjoint locally flat disc fillings from algebraic dual spheres and the vanishing intersection invariants alone. -> The constructed boundary circles satisfy the algebraic input conditions but admit no such filling.
- **anti-zero-graphs-imply-memberships**: Apply the formal nonexistence theorem after checking only complementary tangent spaces and vanishing on the graph. -> The proof additionally requires both cubic gradient-ideal membership laws with coefficients.
- **anti-retraction-image-equals-kernel-subcomplex**: Treat the chain idempotent as a projection whose entire image equals the designated kernel subcomplex. -> The assertion controls its action on the endomorphism kernel; it does not identify its entire image.
- **anti-corrected-graph-is-geometric**: Interpret the corrected evaluation graph as a family of geometric representations. -> The formal correction may mix graph variables and requires no geometric realization.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. This harness checks the finite tensor-kernel construction in the joint-kernels lemma over bounded prime fields. It computes the joint kernel and the sum of the specified tensor subspaces independently. It does not certify the main disc-embedding counterexample or the nonexistence theorem for arbitrary formal series.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with name, prime p, square matrices, and passive dimension w.
# PRECONDITIONS: p in {2,3,5}; one to five nonempty square matrices; total tensor dimension at most four; w positive.
# OUTPUTS: JSON containing bases lhs and rhs in the fixed tensor-coordinate order.
# TIME: Finite matrix construction and modular Gaussian elimination within the declared bounded domain.
# SPACE: Dense matrices and basis vectors within the declared bounded domain.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer arithmetic reduced modulo the declared prime; exact symbolic tensor products.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and exit unsuccessfully.
import sys, json, itertools, math
from sympy import Matrix, eye, kronecker_product, isprime

def rref(rows, width, q):
    a = [[int(x) % q for x in row] for row in rows]
    pivots = []
    r = 0
    for c in range(width):
        k = next((j for j in range(r, len(a)) if a[j][c]), None)
        if k is None:
            continue
        a[r], a[k] = a[k], a[r]
        inv = pow(a[r][c], -1, q)
        a[r] = [(x * inv) % q for x in a[r]]
        for j in range(len(a)):
            if j != r and a[j][c]:
                b = a[j][c]
                a[j] = [(x - b * y) % q for x, y in zip(a[j], a[r])]
        pivots.append(c)
        r += 1
    return a[:r], pivots

def nullspace(rows, q):
    width = len(rows[0])
    a, pivots = rref(rows, width, q)
    basis = []
    for c in range(width):
        if c not in pivots:
            v = [0] * width
            v[c] = 1
            for row, pivot in zip(a, pivots):
                v[pivot] = -row[c] % q
            basis.append(v)
    return basis

def rowbasis(rows, q):
    return rref(rows, len(rows[0]), q)[0]

def standard(n):
    return [[int(i == j) for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]

def tensor_vector(factors):
    out = [1]
    for vector in factors:
        out = [x * y for x in out for y in vector]
    return out

def solve(data):
    q = data['p']
    matrices = data['matrices']
    w = data['w']
    assert type(q) is int and q in (2, 3, 5) and isprime(q)
    assert type(w) is int and w > 0
    assert isinstance(matrices, list) and 1 <= len(matrices) <= 5
    dims = []
    for M in matrices:
        assert isinstance(M, list) and len(M) > 0
        n = len(M)
        assert all(isinstance(row, list) and len(row) == n for row in M)
        assert all(type(x) is int for row in M for x in row)
        dims.append(n)
    n_total = math.prod(dims) * w
    assert n_total <= 4
    d = len(matrices)
    equations = []
    for triple in itertools.combinations(range(d), 3):
        factors = [Matrix(matrices[i]) if i in triple else eye(dims[i]) for i in range(d)]
        factors.append(eye(w))
        operator = kronecker_product(*factors)
        equations.extend([[int(operator[r, c]) % q for c in range(n_total)] for r in range(n_total)])
    lhs = nullspace(equations, q) if equations else standard(n_total)
    kernels = [nullspace(M, q) for M in matrices]
    required = max(0, d - 2)
    generators = []
    for J in itertools.combinations(range(d), required):
        families = [kernels[i] if i in J else standard(dims[i]) for i in range(d)]
        families.append(standard(w))
        for factors in itertools.product(*families):
            generators.append([x % q for x in tensor_vector(factors)])
    rhs = rref(generators, n_total, q)[0]
    return {'lhs': lhs, 'rhs': rhs}

print(json.dumps(solve(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

          "exact_quote": "For every $r\\in\\mathcal R$ there is a punctured torus $B_r$ properly embedded in $V$, with boundary a knot in $\\operatorname{int}Q$. The $B_r$ are mutually disjoint. They have intersecting basis curves $\\alpha_r,\\beta_r$ and the following attachments in $G$:*\n\n1.  *A shallow cap at $\\alpha_r$ ends in a separate parallel copy of the core disc of end $a$.*\n\n2.  *A further punctured torus $S_r$ is attached at $\\beta_r$. Its basis curves $x_r,y_r$ have caps of end types $b,c$, respectively. Its boundary, with the prescribed basing, represents $[z_b^{c_2},z_c^{c_3}]$, and $\\partial B_r$ represents $R_r$.*\n\n3.  *Compressing $S_r$ along $x_r$ with two framed parallels of its cap produces an embedded disc $D_r$. Intersections of $D_r$ with other objects are allowed. The extension of $D_r$ towards $\\beta_r$ has a framing that extends the angular normal-circle vector required to compress $\\beta_r\\times S^1$.*\n\n*Except at the intended attachments, every body and attaching collar is disjoint from every other such piece.           "exact_quote": "Let $(V,\\mathcal D)$ be a finite-dimensional graded complex over a field, and let $S:V\\to V$ have degree zero. No relation between $S$ and $\\mathcal D$ is assumed. Set $$N=\\ker S,\\qquad K=N\\cap\\mathcal D^{-1}N.$$ There are a degree-zero chain idempotent $r$ and a degree-minus-one map $h$ such that $$r(N)=K,\\qquad r|_K=\\operatorname{id},\\qquad\n                  1-r=\\mathcal Dh+h\\mathcal D.\n \\label{frob:retraction-identity}$$ The image of $r$ is not asserted to equal $K$.",

        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-frob-retraction",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-frob-retraction</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The subspace $K$ is a subcomplex: if $x\\in K$, then $\\mathcal Dx\\in N$ and $\\mathcal D^2x=0\\in N$. Choose a graded complement $N=K\\oplus E$. If $e\\in E$ and $\\mathcal De\\in N$, then $e\\in K$, hence $e=0$. Thus $\\mathcal D$ is injective on $E$, and $\\mathcal DE\\cap N=0$. In particular $K\\oplus E\\oplus\\mathcal DE$ is a graded direct sum. Also $E\\oplus\\mathcal DE$ injects into $V/K$: if $e+\\mathcal De'\\in K$, then $\\mathcal De'\\in N$, so $e'=e=0$.\n\nTake any graded vector-space complement $C'$ to that direct sum, and define $$h(\\mathcal De)=e\\quad(e\\in E),\\qquad\n                     h|_{K\\oplus E\\oplus C'}=0.$$ Set $Q=\\mathcal Dh+h\\mathcal D$. The image of $\\mathcal Dh$ lies in $\\mathcal DE$, and the image of $h\\mathcal D$ lies in $E$, even when $\\mathcal DC'$ has components in the other summands. Moreover $Q$ is the identity on $E$ and on $\\mathcal DE$, and is zero on $K$. Consequently $Q^2=Q$. The equation $\\mathcal D^2=0$ gives $$\\mathcal DQ=\\mathcal Dh\\mathcal D=Q\\mathcal D.$$ Now $r=1-Q$ has all the asserted properties. This calculation is why an arbitrary complement is allowed; an arbitrary vector-space projection by itself would not give a chain map. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-frob-retraction"
        }

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-algebraic-duality-without-goodness** (counterexample_scope): Framed algebraic dual spheres and the stated vanishing invariants do not guarantee disjoint locally flat boundary fillings without a fundamental-group hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Invoke an unrestricted disc-embedding procedure using only the algebraic intersection conditions.']
- **limit-particular-counterexample** (counterexample_scope): The geometric theorem constructs a particular forbidden boundary filling; nongoodness does not assert that every disc family in such a group is unfillable. Blocked use: ['Reject every boundary-filling problem solely because its fundamental group is not good.']
- **limit-free-group-identification** (formal_scope): The free-group conclusion follows through subgroup and quotient closure and does not identify the constructed manifold's fundamental group with the nonabelian free group. Blocked use: ['Assign the nonabelian free group as the fundamental group of the constructed manifold without a separate calculation.']
- **limit-surgery-conjunction** (formal_scope): The surgery corollary excludes the conjunction of the unrestricted assertions and does not determine which assertion fails. Blocked use: ['Present the corollary as a disproof of either unrestricted assertion individually.']
- **limit-cap-and-sphere-disjointness** (formal_scope): Bodies and attaching collars are separated, but opposite end cap sheets may cross. Individual embeddedness of each dual sphere does not imply mutual disjointness of the sphere family. Blocked use: ['Treat the capped-stage construction as a simultaneous geometric separation of all surfaces.', 'Replace algebraic duality by an unsupported assertion of disjoint geometric dual spheres.']
- **limit-exterior-intersection-labels** (hypothesis): Intersection cancellation requires equal Wall labels in the exterior's fundamental group as well as opposite signs. The retained unused upper cap supplies the comparison nullhomotopy. Blocked use: ['Cancel crossings from their signs alone.', 'Verify comparison loops only in the ambient manifold before deleting base neighborhoods.', 'Delete the unused upper caps before the label comparison.']
- **limit-protected-actual-disc-images** (hypothesis): The smooth-cut implication assumes actual pairwise disjoint proper locally flat disc embeddings with the prescribed boundary maps. It does not assume a homotopy from those discs to the input caps. Blocked use: ['Extract the forced cuts from immersed fillings or from algebraic intersection data alone.', 'Weaken the prescribed boundary condition without proving a corresponding cut construction.']
- **limit-cut-components-and-torus-incidence** (hypothesis): The cut manifold may be disconnected and walls may include closed components. Each torus-bearing wall component has its specified single boundary torus, and all components remain in the cohomological comparisons. Blocked use: ['Discard closed wall components when computing internal directions.', 'Assume the cut manifold is connected.', 'Reuse the gluing comparison for walls with additional torus attachments without checking the transport conditions.']
- **limit-full-defect-ideal** (formal_scope): The deformation argument retains the entire triangle-defect ideal, including its nonreduced structure. Internal derivatives must generate that full ideal, rather than merely have the same common zero set. Blocked use: ['Replace the defect ideal by its radical.', 'Substitute agreement of zero sets for equality of derivative and defect ideals.']
- **limit-independent-conjugators** (hypothesis): Cubic nilpotence uses the full finite relation family, with independently varying conjugators and repeated end labels. Adjoint spanning alone does not supply the relations. Blocked use: ['Infer cubic nilpotence from the reference matrices alone.', 'Retain only triples with distinct ends or a shared conjugator and claim the same ideal law.']
- **limit-formal-connection-coefficients** (formal_scope): The connection and integration argument uses completed characteristic-zero coefficient modules and closed ideal extensions. It does not construct the original wall connections over a positive-characteristic field. Blocked use: ['Reduce the original integration-defined potentials directly to positive characteristic.', 'Treat completed ideal-valued smooth forms as ordinary finite polynomial data without a justified truncation.']
- **limit-off-equation-collar-commutation** (formal_scope): The prescribed logarithmic collar form preserves the flat reduction, but its off-equation logarithms need not commute. Blocked use: ['Declare the interpolated collar connection flat before imposing the wall defects.', 'Drop commutator curvature terms solely because the reduced torus holonomies commute.']
- **limit-polarization-and-differentiation-order** (hypothesis): The wall function includes the boundary polarization term. Internal derivatives are taken in independent wall coordinates before substitution on the restriction graph. Blocked use: ['Use an unpolarized wall integral in the derivative-ideal argument.', 'Differentiate the composed graph value in place of the independent internal derivatives.']
- **limit-closed-connection-and-gauge-comparisons** (hypothesis): The square-ideal connection comparison requires a closed oriented manifold, ideal-valued curvature and an ideal-valued perturbing one-form. A separate gauge comparison contributes a parameter constant, which need not vanish before reference subtraction. Blocked use: ['Apply the square-ideal comparison to an arbitrary perturbation.', 'Apply the closed-manifold formula unchanged to a wall with boundary.', 'Assume that a reference gauge contributes no constant or extends over the vertex manifold.']
- **limit-fixed-ring-gradient-jets** (approximation): All finite gradient jets have representatives in one fixed local complex algebraic ring. This does not establish algebraicity of the full potential or place every original coefficient in one finitely generated integer algebra. Blocked use: ['Infer algebraicity of the complete original potential from algebraicity of its finite gradient jets.', 'Choose an arithmetic coefficient ring for all original integration coefficients without replacement and witness selection.']
- **limit-primitive-replacement-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Algebraic primitive replacement requires a local étale complex coordinate ring, gradient membership in the ideal and representatives of every gradient jet in that same ring. It imposes no reducedness, primaryness or equidimensionality assumption on the ideal. Blocked use: ['Apply the replacement lemma when gradient representatives exist only in changing chart rings.', 'Extend the characteristic-zero differential argument to arbitrary coefficient fields without proof.', 'Ignore embedded primary components when selecting the Taylor order.']
- **limit-replacement-error-control** (approximation): The stated high-order wall-ideal replacement preserves the value and gradient identities through the pulled-back wall-ideal cube containments. Ordinary maximal-ideal closeness alone is not the stated preservation contract. Blocked use: ['Replace potentials at an arbitrary truncation order and assume all membership witnesses survive.', 'Estimate derivatives of composed functions instead of differentiating wall errors before pullback.']
- **limit-formal-correction-is-not-geometric** (formal_scope): The corrected evaluation graph may mix graph variables and requires no geometric realization. The cubic laws after correction are transported formal ideal memberships. Blocked use: ['Interpret the corrected graph as actual representation restrictions.', 'Reapply the original geometric holonomy law to the corrected graph instead of transporting its witnesses.']
- **limit-specialization-ring-and-prime** (hypothesis): Specialization uses the common coefficient ring of the replacement potentials, corrected graph and membership witnesses, including designated inverses. The source asserts sufficiently large primes without a specified numerical threshold. Blocked use: ['Specialize at an arbitrary prime without preserving the designated units.', 'Reduce the potentials while omitting their membership coefficients.', 'Claim an explicit prime threshold from the qualitative specialization statement.']
- **limit-frobenius-obstruction-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Formal-data nonexistence requires the stated prime-characteristic and wall-label regime, separated potentials, centered graph data, injective complementary tangents, exact graph vanishing and both cubic gradient-ideal membership families. Blocked use: ['Apply the obstruction using only graph vanishing and tangent complementarity.', 'Replace ideal memberships with vanishing on a common zero set.', 'Extend the theorem to an unstated characteristic or wall-label regime.']
- **limit-coordinate-frobenius-complex** (formal_scope): The finite form argument uses separate coordinate Frobenius powers and distinguishes ordinary differentiation from wedge multiplication by the potential differential. Its pairing argument needs nonvanishing in the original coordinates, not an asserted coordinate-invariance formula. Blocked use: ['Replace the coordinate quotient by a different truncation without checking differential descent.', 'Conflate ordinary differential cohomology with Koszul cohomology.', 'Transport the residue as a coordinate-invariant scalar without justification.']
- **limit-chain-retraction-image** (formal_scope): The chain idempotent sends the endomorphism kernel onto its differential-stable part and fixes that part. Its entire image is not asserted to equal that subcomplex. Blocked use: ['Replace the construction by an arbitrary vector-space projection.', 'Identify the entire image of the chain idempotent with the differential-stable kernel.']
- **limit-tensor-kernel-field-and-slot-actions** (hypothesis): The tensor-kernel construction assumes finite-dimensional vector spaces over a field, separate factor endomorphisms and identity actions on passive factors. Injectivity is required on the chosen complementary domains, without requiring their images to avoid the kernels. Blocked use: ['Apply the decomposition directly to modules over an arbitrary ring.', 'Allow operators to couple tensor slots or act nontrivially on passive factors.', 'Require an unsupported direct-sum decomposition of the operator images and kernels.']
- **limit-finite-tests-and-global-results** (nonconstructive): Exact checks of supplied finite tensor instances verify those instances. They do not certify the geometric counterexample, the arbitrary-field tensor theorem or the nonexistence theorem for unrestricted formal series.           "exact_quote": "The ends of each cut can be designated $+$ and $-$, and torus coordinates $(\\theta_i,\\eta_i)$ of period one can be chosen, so that the image of $$H^1(J_i;\\mathbb C)\\longrightarrow H^1(\\partial J_i;\\mathbb C)$$ is the $\\theta_i$-axis. In $Q$, the $\\eta_i$-circle bounds on the positive side and the $\\theta_i$-circle bounds on the negative side. Put $$n_i=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H^1(J_i,\\partial J_i;\\mathfrak l),\n \\qquad D=\\sum_i n_i,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal Y=\\bigoplus_i(\\mathbb C^{n_i}\\oplus\\mathbb C^{n_i}).$$ Then $\\dim U=3d+D$. The joint first-slope restriction $U\\to\\mathfrak l^d$ is onto. Its kernel $U_0$ has dimension $D$. The two maps from $U_0$ to $\\mathcal Y$ given by the paired restrictions and by their interchange are injective and have complementary images.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        },

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-conn-value-ideal",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-conn-value-ideal</summary>\n\n*Proof.* All wall connections in this proof are first pulled back by their maps $\\iota_i^\\pm$ to $B=\\mathbb C[[a,t]]$. Their curvature lies in $\\mathfrak r$. On a positive wall the second torus slope bounds in $Q$, so $V_i(a_i,Y_i^+)\\in\\mathfrak r$. Choose a fiber cutoff $\\chi$ supported on its collar, equal to one near the boundary, and put $$A_{i,u}=A_i-u\\chi V_i\\,d\\eta_i,\\qquad 0\\leq u\\leq1 .$$ The connection change lies in $\\mathfrak r$, and every interpolated curvature also lies in $\\mathfrak r$. This is immediate from the curvature change formula and does not assume commutation away from the defect quotient. Use the polarized functional along this path: $$P_{i,u}=\\operatorname{CS}_{J_i}(A_{i,u})\n                  +\\epsilon_i\\operatorname{tr}(U_i(1-u)V_i).$$ Here $U_i$ is held fixed. Formula <a href=\"#conn:variation-formula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conn:variation-formula\">[conn:variation-formula]</a> has zero boundary term for the $u$-variation; its bulk term is a product of two $\\mathfrak r$-valued factors. Thus $P_{i,1}-P_{i,0}\\in\\mathfrak r^2$. At $u=1$ the polarization correction is zero and the collar form is the pure form $U_i\\,d\\theta_i$. On a negative wall $U_i(0)=0$ already, so its functional is raw Chern–Simons and its collar form is the pure form $V_i(0,Y_i^-)\\,d\\eta_i$. It follows that, modulo $\\mathfrak r^2$, $\\Psi(a,Y)$ is the oriented sum of the raw wall Chern–Simons integrals of these modified connections.\n\nWe next construct an *exactly flat* formal connection on $Q$ with those pure collar forms. Its reduction must be the actual restriction from the flat vertex family, including based transports. Fix the standard paths from the main basepoint of $Q$ to the seam basepoints. Lift their comparison transport matrices from $B/\\mathfrak r$ to $B$. Assign each free end generator the required pure collar holonomy, conjugated by its lifted comparison transport and adjusted for its orientation. The other slope at that seam bounds in $Q$. Lift the matrices of the $h$-generators from the vertex restriction arbitrarily in $\\operatorname{SL}_2(B)$. Such lifts exist because the group is smooth at every reference matrix. Since the end generators and $h$-generators freely generate $\\pi_1(Q)$, these choices impose no relations and define a representation over $B$ lifting the specified one over $B/\\mathfrak r$. Its associated flat bundle has an exactly flat connection. Choose its frame at each seam basepoint using the lifted comparison transport; parallel transport followed by a one-slope exponential gives the prescribed pure connection form on that collar.\n\nThese collar frames extend to a global frame on $Q$. At the origin this is a relative section problem for a special-linear bundle on a compact three-manifold, with prescribed section on its boundary collars and at its basepoint. The group $\\operatorname{SL}_2(\\mathbb C)$ retracts onto $\\operatorname{SU}_2\\cong S^3$, so its $\\pi_0,\\pi_1,\\pi_2$ vanish. The obstruction to extending a section across a relative cell of dimension $j\\leq3$ lies in $\\pi_{j-1}$ of this group; all such obstructions vanish. A smooth reference extension follows by relative smooth approximation. At successive formal orders the discrepancy is identity modulo the maximal ideal and extends coefficientwise with a cutoff, just as in Lemma <a href=\"#conn:wall-collar\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conn:wall-collar\">21</a>. This yields a global formal frame agreeing with every prescribed collar frame.\n\nIn this frame $\\operatorname{CS}_Q$ is constant in the parameters. Its variation has zero bulk term by exact flatness. On each torus both its connection form and its variation lie along the same single one-form $d\\theta_i$ or $d\\eta_i$, so $\\operatorname{tr}(A_Q\\wedge\\delta A_Q)$ is zero. The boundary term of the raw Chern–Simons variation therefore also vanishes.\n\nGlue these framed pieces to form a connection $A_{\\partial}$ in a global frame on the closed three-manifold $\\partial X$. The forms agree on the product collars, so their integrals add. We must compare the assembled flat reduction with the actual restriction from $X$, including its transports. The component $X_0=X_Q$ containing $Q$ contains all the torus-bearing wall copies in its boundary. Each such copy attaches to $Q$ along exactly one connected torus. The incidence graph has one $Q$-vertex and one leaf for each such wall copy. It has no cycles. Fix the flat comparison on $Q$, including its seam-basepoint values. On a wall its based representation is the prescribed restriction, so choose the flat comparison with that same value at its single seam basepoint. Parallel transport makes the two comparisons agree on the entire connected torus. There is no second attachment imposing an additional transport condition. Every closed wall component is an isolated boundary piece for this purpose and is compared separately, whether it belongs to $X_0$ or to another component of $X$. Thus the assembled reduction is isomorphic to the full boundary restriction of the vertex family.\n\nLift this boundary bundle isomorphism formally. In global frames this amounts to lifting smooth matrix coefficients; the reference determinant is nonzero, so the lifted matrix has a formal inverse. No extension of this identification over $X$ is required. Denote by $g$ the resulting boundary gauge comparison with the already chosen global frame of $A_X$. Then in a common boundary frame $$A_{\\partial}=A_X^g|_{\\partial X}+\\eta,\n                         \\qquad \\eta\\in\\mathfrak r .$$ Both compared curvatures are in $\\mathfrak r$. The first part of Lemma <a href=\"#conn:closed-comparisons\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conn:closed-comparisons\">26</a> shows that this *connection change* contributes only an element of $\\mathfrak r^2$. The second part shows that the separate *frame change* contributes a parameter-constant term, which disappears after subtracting reference values. The gauge $g(0)$ may have nonzero degree; it is not asserted to extend over $X$.\n\nFinally apply Stokes in the global frame already chosen on $X$. The identity $$d\\,\\operatorname{tr}\n      \\left(A_X\\wedge dA_X+\\frac23 A_X^3\\right)\n                 =\\operatorname{tr}(F_{A_X}\\wedge F_{A_X})$$ follows by the graded cyclic trace rule. Hence $$\\operatorname{CS}_{\\partial X}(A_X|_{\\partial X})\n       =\\int_X\\operatorname{tr}(F_{A_X}\\wedge F_{A_X})\n                                                  \\in\\mathfrak r^2.$$ Stokes applies componentwise to the compact oriented smooth four-manifold with its rounded collared corners, including any components with empty boundary. The $Q$-contribution is constant, all $P_i(0)$ are zero, and the preceding connection and gauge comparisons hold after subtracting the origin values. These facts prove <a href=\"#conn:value-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conn:value-identity\">[conn:value-identity]</a>. ◻",

          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a compact smooth manifold, possibly disconnected and with boundary, and let $\\rho_Z$ be a fixed $\\operatorname{SL}_2(\\mathbb C)$-local system. There is an algebraic family of edge matrices on a finite good cover, with parameters $T$, having these properties:*\n\n1.  *Its reference value is the chosen local system, its tangent is the specified inclusion of $T$, and its triangle-defect ideal $\\mathfrak r_Z$ is contained in $\\mathfrak m^2$.*\n\n2.  *Modulo $\\mathfrak r_Z$, its matrices are the transitions of an exact flat family.*\n\n3.  *For a nonzero functional $\\lambda:\\mathfrak r_Z/\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak r_Z\\to\\mathbb C$, let $\\mathfrak s_\\lambda\\subset\\mathfrak r_Z$ be the inverse image of $\\ker\\lambda$.           "exact_quote": "Write $[u,v]=uvu^{-1}v^{-1}$ and $z^c=czc^{-1}$. Let $F_h$ be free on the three named generators $h_0,h_+,h_-$. Take $\\mathcal H$ to be all reduced words of length at most five in $h_0^{\\pm1},h_+^{\\pm1},h_-^{\\pm1}$, including the empty word. This is a finite set closed under inversion and reversal. Put $$\\mathcal R=(\\mathcal E\\times\\mathcal H)^3.$$ Thus $k=|\\mathcal R|=(2d\\,|\\mathcal H|)^3$ is finite. The entry $r=((a,c_1),(b,c_2),(c,c_3))$ specifies the word $$\\label{geo:relations}\n R_r=[z_a^{c_1},[z_b^{c_2},z_c^{c_3}]].$$ All triples occur, including repetitions, and the three conjugators vary independently.",

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-def-cubic-nilpotence",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-def-cubic-nilpotence</summary>\n\n*Proof.* All computations initially take place in the Noetherian local ring $B/\\mathfrak r$. Let $Z=z-1$ for an end matrix. The determinant equation gives $\\operatorname{tr}Z=-\\det Z\\in\\mathfrak n^2$. Thus one may replace $Z$ by its traceless part when computing triple products modulo $\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak n^3$. Indeed the discarded products lie in $\\mathfrak n^4\\subset\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak n^3$.\n\nThe formal group-commutator expansion is $$[1+A,[1+B,1+C]]\n     =1+[A,[B,C]]+\\text{terms of total degree at least four}.$$ It follows by expanding each inverse as $(1+A)^{-1}=1-A+A^2-\\cdots$; the inner commutator starts with $1+[B,C]$, and its commutator with $1+A$ first contributes $[A,[B,C]]$. In <a href=\"#def:relations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"def:relations\">[def:relations]</a>, replacing a conjugating matrix by its reference value changes the displayed cubic term only by $\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak n^3$. Every relation therefore implies, in $\\mathfrak n^3/\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak n^3$, $$[\\operatorname{Ad}\\rho(c_1)Z_1,\n   [\\operatorname{Ad}\\rho(c_2)Z_2,\n    \\operatorname{Ad}\\rho(c_3)Z_3]]=0,$$ with traceless $Z_j$. The independent choice of all three conjugators, followed by linear combination and Lemma <a href=\"#geo:spanning\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:spanning\">18</a>, replaces the three adjoint maps by arbitrary endomorphisms of $\\mathfrak l$.\n\nFor any three scalar linear functionals $\\ell_1,\\ell_2,\\ell_3$ on $\\mathfrak l$, choose these endomorphisms as $$x\\longmapsto\\ell_1(x)E,\\qquad\n x\\longmapsto\\ell_2(x)H,\\qquad\n x\\longmapsto\\ell_3(x)F,$$ where $$E=\\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\\\0&0\\end{pmatrix},\\quad\n H=\\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\\\0&-1\\end{pmatrix},\\quad\n F=\\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\\\1&0\\end{pmatrix}.$$ Since $[E,[H,F]]=-2H\\ne0$, every product $\\ell_1(Z_1)\\ell_2(Z_2)\\ell_3(Z_3)$ vanishes in this quotient. The relation list permits repeated ends, so this includes all generators of $\\mathfrak n^3/\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak n^3$. Hence $\\mathfrak n^3=\\mathfrak m\\mathfrak n^3$; Nakayama’s lemma, applied to the finitely generated ideal $\\mathfrak n^3$, proves <a href=\"#def:n-cube\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"def:n-cube\">[def:n-cube]</a>.\n\nUnder either restriction, the wall defects vanish modulo $\\mathfrak r$. Each slope holonomy is either $1$ or the holonomy of its end longitude, with a possible inverse and a change of basing. Entries of its deviation therefore belong to $\\mathfrak n$. This remains true for inverses, since $z^{-1}-1=-z^{-1}(z-1)$, and for conjugation. The images of all the $\\mathfrak k_i$ are consequently contained in $\\mathfrak n$, proving the final statements. ◻",

          "anchor": "stmt-def-coefficient-convention",
          "exact_quote": "An algebraic parameter chart will mean a local $\\mathbb C$-algebra essentially of finite type and etale over an affine coordinate space at the designated origin. Its completion is $B=\\mathbb C[[v_1,\\ldots,v_N]]$, with maximal ideal $\\mathfrak m$. Finite collections of algebraic germs obtained by an implicit equation with invertible Jacobian can always be placed in one such chart, after replacing it by a common etale neighborhood.\n\nFor a smooth manifold $Z$, smooth forms with formal coefficients mean $$\\Omega^j(Z)\\widehat\\otimes B\n   =\\varprojlim_k\\bigl(\\Omega^j(Z)\\otimes_{\\mathbb C}\n                                     B/\\mathfrak m^k\\bigr).$$ For quotients of $B$, use the analogous inverse limit. The extension of a scalar ideal $I$ to this space always means its closed extension. Differential operations in the $Z$-variables preserve that extension; multiplication respects its powers. Integration over a compact manifold is defined at each finite parameter quotient and then by inverse limit.",

        },

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-alg-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-alg-specialization</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Because $R\\subset\\mathbb C$, the finite type $\\mathbb Q$-algebra $R_{\\mathbb Q}=R\\otimes_{\\mathbb Z}\\mathbb Q$ is nonzero. Choose a maximal ideal. The weak Nullstellensatz, or Zariski’s lemma, says that its residue field $L$ is a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q$. We obtain a homomorphism $R\\to L$. The images of the finitely many ring generators, including all designated inverses, lie in $\\mathcal O_L[1/N]$ for some positive integer $N$: multiplying each algebraic number by a suitable nonzero integer makes it integral. Thus the homomorphism factors as $$R\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_L[1/N]\\longrightarrow\n       \\mathcal O_L/\\mathfrak l=\\Bbbk$$ for any prime ideal $\\mathfrak l$ above a rational prime $\\ell\\nmid N$. The residue field is finite. Every designated determinant stays a unit, because its inverse is already in $R$.\n\nCoefficientwise reduction commutes with multiplication, centered substitution, and formal differentiation; each coefficient involves only a finite sum and differentiation multiplies by an integer. It therefore preserves every identity in <a href=\"#alg:seven\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:seven\">[alg:seven]</a>, including the membership coefficients, and the unit tangent determinant. It also preserves the order-at-least-three condition and the separated formula for $\\Psi$. The formal inverse construction in the theorem reduces over the same ring, giving the claimed smooth zero graphs. Only the algebraic replacements and their subsequent formal recursions are reduced. The original complex Chern–Simons series, and all integration used to obtain them, are absent from this specialization step. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-conn-wall-collar",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-conn-wall-collar</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Temporarily write $U,V,\\mathfrak d$ for the wall data. Modulo $\\mathfrak d$ the two torus holonomies commute. Their formal logarithms commute as well: they are power series in the two commuting matrices. On the simply connected cover of the collar, take the flat parallel frame $P$ issuing from the prescribed based frame. In the chosen convention the frame $$P\\exp(\\theta U+\\eta V)$$ is periodic, since the exponential cancels the deck holonomies $\\exp(-U)$, $\\exp(-V)$. Its fiber connection form is $U\\,d\\theta+V\\,d\\eta$.\n\nTo lift it, use finitely many small collar patches with lifted torus coordinates. On each patch a lift of $P$ is expressed using an image frame $S_\\alpha$ and a fixed finite transition word. Multiply by the displayed exponential. These local lifted frames have the same reduction modulo $\\mathfrak d$; their partition-of-unity average is therefore a formal frame with that reduction. In this frame prescribe <a href=\"#conn:collar\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conn:collar\">[conn:collar]</a> near the boundary, including its zero parameter components, and use a collar cutoff to interpolate with the previous total connection. Their fiber reductions are the same flat connection. Thus the interpolation preserves the reduction and keeps the fiber curvature in $\\mathfrak d$. This reasoning does not require the off-equation matrices $U,V$ to commute.\n\nAt the origin the representation on each wall component is trivial, and the based choices make the collar frame the restriction of a global parallel frame. Lift that frame globally as in Lemma <a href=\"#conn:image-bundle\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conn:image-bundle\">20</a>. On a collar its discrepancy from the prescribed frame is a matrix $G=1+O(\\mathfrak m)$. Multiplication of the global frame by $1+\\chi(G-1)$, extending coefficients by zero using a collar cutoff $\\chi$, makes it agree with the prescribed frame on a shorter collar. This matrix is formally invertible. The resulting global frame has the asserted properties. Closed wall components require only the initial global parallel frame and its formal lift. ◻",

          "anchor": "stmt-conn-closed-comparisons",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N$ be a closed oriented three-manifold, and let $A$ be a global formal connection with $F_A\\in I$, for a scalar ideal $I$.*\n\n1.  *If $\\eta\\in I$ is a one-form, then $$\\operatorname{CS}_N(A+\\eta)-\\operatorname{CS}_N(A)\\in I^2.$$*\n\n2.  *For an arbitrary smooth formal gauge $g$, $$\\operatorname{CS}_N(A^g)-\\operatorname{CS}_N(A)$$ is independent of the parameters. The reference gauge need not be nullhomotopic.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-conn-value-ideal",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-conn-value-ideal</summary>\n\n*Proof.* All wall connections in this proof are first pulled back by their maps $\\iota_i^\\pm$ to $B=\\mathbb C[[a,t]]$. Their curvature lies in $\\mathfrak r$. On a positive wall the second torus slope bounds in $Q$, so $V_i(a_i,Y_i^+)\\in\\mathfrak r$. Choose a fiber cutoff $\\chi$ supported on its collar, equal to one near the boundary, and put $$A_{i,u}=A_i-u\\chi V_i\\,d\\eta_i,\\qquad 0\\leq u\\leq1 .$$ The connection change lies in $\\mathfrak r$, and every interpolated curvature also lies in $\\mathfrak r$. This is immediate from the curvature change formula and does not assume commutation away from the defect quotient. Use the polarized functional along this path: $$P_{i,u}=\\operatorname{CS}_{J_i}(A_{i,u})\n                  +\\epsilon_i\\operatorname{tr}(U_i(1-u)V_i).$$ Here $U_i$ is held fixed. Formula <a href=\"#conn:variation-formula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conn:variation-formula\">[conn:variation-formula]</a> has zero boundary term for the $u$-variation; its bulk term is a product of two $\\mathfrak r$-valued factors. Thus $P_{i,1}-P_{i,0}\\in\\mathfrak r^2$. At $u=1$ the polarization correction is zero and the collar form is the pure form $U_i\\,d\\theta_i$. On a negative wall $U_i(0)=0$ already, so its functional is raw Chern–Simons and its collar form is the pure form $V_i(0,Y_i^-)\\,d\\eta_i$. It follows that, modulo $\\mathfrak r^2$, $\\Psi(a,Y)$ is the oriented sum of the raw wall Chern–Simons integrals of these modified connections.\n\nWe next construct an *exactly flat* formal connection on $Q$ with those pure collar forms. Its reduction must be the actual restriction from the flat vertex family, including based transports. Fix the standard paths from the main basepoint of $Q$ to the seam basepoints. Lift their comparison transport matrices from $B/\\mathfrak r$ to $B$. Assign each free end generator the required pure collar holonomy, conjugated by its lifted comparison transport and adjusted for its orientation. The other slope at that seam bounds in $Q$. Lift the matrices of the $h$-generators from the vertex restriction arbitrarily in $\\operatorname{SL}_2(B)$. Such lifts exist because the group is smooth at every reference matrix. Since the end generators and $h$-generators freely generate $\\pi_1(Q)$, these choices impose no relations and define a representation over $B$ lifting the specified one over $B/\\mathfrak r$. Its associated flat bundle has an exactly flat connection. Choose its frame at each seam basepoint using the lifted comparison transport; parallel transport followed by a one-slope exponential gives the prescribed pure connection form on that collar.\n\nThese collar frames extend to a global frame on $Q$. At the origin this is a relative section problem for a special-linear bundle on a compact three-manifold, with prescribed section on its boundary collars and at its basepoint. The group $\\operatorname{SL}_2(\\mathbb C)$ retracts onto $\\operatorname{SU}_2\\cong S^3$, so its $\\pi_0,\\pi_1,\\pi_2$ vanish. The obstruction to extending a section across a relative cell of dimension $j\\leq3$ lies in $\\pi_{j-1}$ of this group; all such obstructions vanish. A smooth reference extension follows by relative smooth approximation. At successive formal orders the discrepancy is identity modulo the maximal ideal and extends coefficientwise with a cutoff, just as in Lemma <a href=\"#conn:wall-collar\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conn:wall-collar\">21</a>. This yields a global formal frame agreeing with every prescribed collar frame.\n\nIn this frame $\\operatorname{CS}_Q$ is constant in the parameters. Its variation has zero bulk term by exact flatness. On each torus both its connection form and its variation lie along the same single one-form $d\\theta_i$ or $d\\eta_i$, so $\\operatorname{tr}(A_Q\\wedge\\delta A_Q)$ is zero. The boundary term of the raw Chern–Simons variation therefore also vanishes.\n\nGlue these framed pieces to form a connection $A_{\\partial}$ in a global frame on the closed three-manifold $\\partial X$. The forms agree on the product collars, so their integrals add. We must compare the assembled flat reduction with the actual restriction from $X$, including its transports. The component $X_0=X_Q$ containing $Q$ contains all the torus-bearing wall copies in its boundary. Each such copy attaches to $Q$ along exactly one connected torus. The incidence graph has one $Q$-vertex and one leaf for each such wall copy. It has no cycles. Fix the flat comparison on $Q$, including its seam-basepoint values. On a wall its based representation is the prescribed restriction, so choose the flat comparison with that same value at its single seam basepoint. Parallel transport makes the two comparisons agree on the entire connected torus. There is no second attachment imposing an additional transport condition. Every closed wall component is an isolated boundary piece for this purpose and is compared separately, whether it belongs to $X_0$ or to another component of $X$. Thus the assembled reduction is isomorphic to the full boundary restriction of the vertex family.\n\nLift this boundary bundle isomorphism formally. In global frames this amounts to lifting smooth matrix coefficients; the reference determinant is nonzero, so the lifted matrix has a formal inverse. No extension of this identification over $X$ is required. Denote by $g$ the resulting boundary gauge comparison with the already chosen global frame of $A_X$. Then in a common boundary frame $$A_{\\partial}=A_X^g|_{\\partial X}+\\eta,\n                         \\qquad \\eta\\in\\mathfrak r .$$ Both compared curvatures are in $\\mathfrak r$. The first part of Lemma <a href=\"#conn:closed-comparisons\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"conn:closed-comparisons\">26</a> shows that this *connection change* contributes only an element of $\\mathfrak r^2$. The second part shows that the separate *frame change* contributes a parameter-constant term, which disappears after subtracting reference values. The gauge $g(0)$ may have nonzero degree; it is not asserted to extend over $X$.\n\nFinally apply Stokes in the global frame already chosen on $X$. The identity $$d\\,\\operatorname{tr}\n      \\left(A_X\\wedge dA_X+\\frac23 A_X^3\\right)\n                 =\\operatorname{tr}(F_{A_X}\\wedge F_{A_X})$$ follows by the graded cyclic trace rule. Hence $$\\operatorname{CS}_{\\partial X}(A_X|_{\\partial X})\n       =\\int_X\\operatorname{tr}(F_{A_X}\\wedge F_{A_X})\n                                                  \\in\\mathfrak r^2.$$ Stokes applies componentwise to the compact oriented smooth four-manifold with its rounded collared corners, including any components with empty boundary. The $Q$-contribution is constant, all $P_i(0)$ are zero, and the preceding connection and gauge comparisons hold after subtracting the origin values. These facts prove <a href=\"#conn:value-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"conn:value-identity\">[conn:value-identity]</a>. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"

      "blocked_use": [
        "Apply the replacement lemma when gradient representatives exist only in changing chart rings.",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-alg-replacement",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be the local ring at a complex point of an étale neighborhood of affine $n$-space, with coordinates $v$ centered at that point, and let $B=\\widehat A=\\mathbb C[[v]]$. Let $K$ be any ideal of $A$. Suppose $P\\in B$ satisfies $$\\partial_{v_j}P\\in KB,\\qquad\n \\partial_{v_j}P\\in A+K^bB\n \\quad\\text{for every $j$ and every $b\\geq1$}.\n \\label{alg:replacement-hyp}$$ For every $m\\geq1$ there is $P_m\\in A$ such that $P-P_m\\in K^mB$. No reducedness, primaryness, or equidimensionality condition is imposed on $K$.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-alg-embedded-example",
          "exact_quote": "The differential test in <a href=\"#alg:differential-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:differential-order\">[alg:differential-order]</a> measures order in the normal directions at each prime $\\mathfrak p$, including nonclosed primes. In the regular local ring $A_{\\mathfrak p}$ the normal cotangent space $\\mathfrak pA_{\\mathfrak p}/(\\mathfrak pA_{\\mathfrak p})^2$ injects into $\\Omega_{A/\\mathbb C}\\otimes_A\\kappa(\\mathfrak p)$. The conormal sequence is injective here because smoothness and separability give its image dimension $\\operatorname{ht}\\mathfrak p$, the dimension of that normal space (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tags 00TT, 00NO, and 00TU) [[Stacks](#ref-Stacks)]. The coordinate derivations therefore span the dual normal directions over $\\kappa(\\mathfrak p)$. A nonzero initial form of degree $j$ in the symmetric graded ring of $A_{\\mathfrak p}$ is detected by $j$ such directions: in characteristic zero a nonzero degree-$j$ polynomial has a nonzero $j$-fold polarized derivative. Lift their coefficients to $A_{\\mathfrak p}$ and expand the resulting composition of derivations. It is an $A_{\\mathfrak p}$-linear combination of coordinate derivatives of orders at most $j$. Thus the vanishing of all derivatives of orders less than $q$ rules out an initial degree $j<q$. This is the local normal-order content of <a href=\"#alg:differential-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:differential-order\">[alg:differential-order]</a>; the possibly singular quotient $A/\\mathfrak p$ at the original closed point causes no difficulty.\n\nThe embedded components also affect the required Taylor order. For $A=\\mathbb C[x,y,z]_{(x,y,z)}$ and $K=(x^2,xy)$ one has $$\\sqrt K=(x),\\qquad\n K^2=(x^4,x^3y,x^2y^2)=(x^2)\\cap(x,y)^4.$$ Here $L_q=(x^q)$. Taylor order $q=4$ suffices for $K^2$, whereas $q=2$ or $3$ does not. The second component is primary for the embedded prime $(x,y)$, whose residue field after localization is $\\mathbb C(z)$. The proof used neither a closed-point residue field at that prime nor an identification of global symbolic and ordinary powers.",
          "source_id": "PM-305.1"
        }

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-alg-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-alg-specialization</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Because $R\\subset\\mathbb C$, the finite type $\\mathbb Q$-algebra $R_{\\mathbb Q}=R\\otimes_{\\mathbb Z}\\mathbb Q$ is nonzero. Choose a maximal ideal. The weak Nullstellensatz, or Zariski’s lemma, says that its residue field $L$ is a finite extension of $\\mathbb Q$. We obtain a homomorphism $R\\to L$. The images of the finitely many ring generators, including all designated inverses, lie in $\\mathcal O_L[1/N]$ for some positive integer $N$: multiplying each algebraic number by a suitable nonzero integer makes it integral. Thus the homomorphism factors as $$R\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_L[1/N]\\longrightarrow\n       \\mathcal O_L/\\mathfrak l=\\Bbbk$$ for any prime ideal $\\mathfrak l$ above a rational prime $\\ell\\nmid N$. The residue field is finite. Every designated determinant stays a unit, because its inverse is already in $R$.\n\nCoefficientwise reduction commutes with multiplication, centered substitution, and formal differentiation; each coefficient involves only a finite sum and differentiation multiplies by an integer. It therefore preserves every identity in <a href=\"#alg:seven\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"alg:seven\">[alg:seven]</a>, including the membership coefficients, and the unit tangent determinant.           "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-frob-normal-cycle</summary>\n\n*Proof.* A centered formal coordinate change and its inverse preserve the coordinate Frobenius ideals. Its ordinary differential is zero because every term differentiating its coefficient repeats one of its normal differentials. Write $F(t,u)\\in(u)$. The tangential coefficients of $\\mathrm dF$ belong to $(u)$, whereas each normal differential wedges to zero with the full normal volume. Therefore $\\delta\\gamma=0$.\n\n\nThe difference $g-\\sum_\\nu\\widetilde h_\\nu\\partial_{C_\\nu}F$ lies in $(u)$ before truncation and continues to do so after truncation. The contraction identity in the original coordinates is the coefficient-linear extension of the exterior and interior product identity in (Koszul 1950, sec. 2, p. 72, (2.9)) [[Koszul1950](#ref-Koszul1950)]: $$\\delta\\iota_\\nu+\\iota_\\nu\\delta\n                         =(\\partial_{C_\\nu}F)\\operatorname{id}.\n \\label{frob:contraction}$$ Since $\\delta$ is linear over $T$, annihilation of $(u)$ and $\\delta\\gamma=0$ prove <a href=\"#frob:primitive-general\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"frob:primitive-general\">[frob:primitive-general]</a>.           "quote": "Let $\\Bbbk$ be a field of prime characteristic $\\ell>2$, and let $d\\geq5$.
